# BACKLOG_DONE: closed backlog items, by sweep

The archive of `BACKLOG.md`'s "Done since the last sweep" list. At each
sweep, `module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py` moves every entry here,
text unchanged, under a dated heading; the newest sweep is on top.

IDs here are never reused. The next free ID for a prefix is the highest
number in `BACKLOG.md` or in this file, plus one:
`python module_work/backlog_dashboard/sweep_done.py --next GM`.

## Swept 2026-11-04 (app master a804998)

- **REF-48** (done, 2026-10-31, geotech-references main 1f7cac5; reviewed independently): a short section outranked the capacity methods in reference search. The cause was not length (eight length priors tried, each lowered MRR and dropped a genuine 85-token answer from the top 5): when no section holds every query word the search falls back to OR, and BM25 put a section holding four of the six words above two holding five. The OR fallback's top 50 hits are now ordered by how many query concepts each holds (a word and its synonyms count once), BM25 breaking ties; AND-mode results never move (4,710 title queries checked). Gold set: auto R@5 41 -> 42 and MRR .559 -> .563, off/fill/rerank unchanged, no case leaves the top 5 in any mode; the CPT case rises from rank 4 to 2. Follow-up REF-53. Noted, not fixed: GEC-6 5.3.5 and Micropile 4.3.5 rank high at median length, so this does not touch them; GEC-5 8.10.2 has no section (REF-52).

- **GM-292** (done, 2026-10-31, merge 3f8f835 with review fixes 247cbae, manual 436bb4c; reviewed and re-checked, 228 recorded inputs replayed unchanged with no water table, 51 made-up walls replayed over the fixes): cantilever and gravity walls take an optional water table behind the wall (`water_table_depth`, `gamma_sat_backfill`, `base_uplift`, `uplift_in_bearing`; `module_work/gm292/CHECK.md`). Below the water table the active pressure uses the buoyant weight and the water pressure is added at a coefficient of 1 (UFC 3-220-20 Sec. 4-4.1 and Fig. 4-16(a), NHI-06-089 Eq. 10-10 and Fig. 10-12); the soil over the heel counts at its saturated weight below the water (EM 1110-2-2104 p. 18); the uplift on the base varies linearly from the heel's head to the toe's (EM 1110-2-2104 para. E-12, p. 115; uniform as an option, its same-water-level case, p. 116) and stays out of the bearing pressures by default (UFC p. 292, the safe side while e <= B/6; EN 1997-1 6.5.2.1(3)P for the option that counts it); gamma_sat defaults to gamma as UFC Fig. 4-16(a) does, its direction stated in the choice. NHI Example 10-1's printed pressures reproduced within 0.12 %; UFC Fig. 4-16(a)'s equations (the drawn curve reads up to 0.04 above them). A made-up 5 m wall with water 2 m down: sliding FOS 1.20 -> 0.725, overturning 3.32 -> 1.77. Owner question OWN-117 (where the uplift's moment counts). Noted, not fixed: tailwater and seepage under the wall (UFC Fig. 4-16(c)) are not modelled; water in a tension crack (NHI p. 10-15) is not added; the water table is level (NHI Ex 10-1's parallel-to-slope table is the same at the face); the MSE wall's external checks have no water table; a wall the uplift floats (net V <= 0) reports e = 0 and passes the eccentricity check while sliding and overturning still fail.

Smith viscous damping acts through the whole blow; the time step is divided by 1.6; toe damping 0.49 s/m; steel 77.3 kN/m3; refusal 120 blows/ft (240 on hard rock, p. 171), was about 914. Run 5's give-ups were read as rate limits only because the FDE's glue raised its own RateLimitGaveUp; the app's Foundry layer (`webapp/palantir_sdk_engine.py`) retries that name for five minutes and then raises the error as it came, which `is_rate_limit` did not recognise (none of its words), so a give-up on the transform route would have been recorded and paced as a refused request. Now the name (and a QoS exception) counts, in the message or on `error_name`; made-up test in test_ri139.py; 305 tests (engine, Prompter, chat-model, RI-139, palantir engine). The cause was planlens' `_exploration_id`, which read a results sheet's letterhead ('Tel' and a number of space-separated groups, folded to the shape of an id) as the log item's title, quoted to the log model as 'The document titles it'. planlens now sets aside every top-band line holding a telephone or fax word before a number and skips a match headed by a letterhead word (TEL, FAX, PHONE, BOX and kin); report ingest ignores a phone-shaped item title in the brief and the split while planlens 0.13 runs; `log_holes` refuses phone lines as defence. One saved report (R19) offline: items titled from the phone line 8 -> 0, briefs quoting it 8 -> 0, no other item's pages or title changed; 3 of the 8 now take a generic title-block word as their title (PL-34).

Offline: the saved R13/R16 negatives go to 0. Follow-ups RI-153 (a top just above the surface whose base is below it, up to the scorer's tolerance), RI-154 (elevation-only lab twins; one snap note per log). Also 7dd8481: three of RI-117's class-scoped test fixtures made classmethods for pytest 10.
- **RI-143** (lab summary rows and sheets the match could not place): 2026-10-27 (3f66984, squash of wip/ri143, reviewed three rounds). A summary row with no boring takes its hole from an `other` column whose heading names a hole (sample/depth/date headings refused), and rows under a merged cell inherit it only when the next hole's log has no sample at their depth; a unitless depth is read in the record's one depth unit (`depth_unit`; mixed units = none) and a depth printed only in a sample label is read, for linking and matching only; percent solids, total solids, dry matter and a resistivity test's minimum-at moisture are never water content, and a water content equal to a solids value is refused unless S is 50 (wet basis) or about 61.8 (dry basis), the only values where the definitions allow it (`lab_floor.solids_can_equal_water`); test names inside brackets kept, units and element symbols ('Potassium (K)') stripped; composite or joined labels ('BH-47/48', 'BH-47 & 48', a second hole of another series) link neither hole; one shared sample numbering only where a row and a sheet in one hole at one depth agree and no number repeats across holes or depths; an unlogged hole matched by its own name; the checklist calls a kind the record holds no sheet of a note (bound reports read on their own -> unsure). Follow-ups RI-149, RI-150, RI-151.
- **RI-117** (pages of several holes read as one hole): 2026-10-27 (ee80b5f, squash of wip/ri117, reviewed twice). `report_ingest/log_holes.py` names each log page's hole as an engineer reads the header: a hole field's value (beside, then under, the nearest line and only if it is a hole code), a title with the value beside it ('Log of Boring', 'Test Pit Log'), '#'/'No.' between series and number, a name standing alone only where nothing else names the page (never sample/run designations, months, four-digit codes, logos or machine codes, page-part words, numbers with units or 'of N'; one-letter series rising one per page read as plate numbers); `split_log_items` cuts an item only where a new hole starts (continuation sheets and form changes kept by `names_match`), so each part is one hole; the log floor reads only its own hole's pages; `missing_holes_qa` lists holes a field or title names that the record lacks; `_ingest_steps` lists a superseded whole-item file (and '-own' files) apart. - **RI-142** (the lab checklist took any sheet as covering a row): 2026-10-27 (merge of 3a5fd4c + ef09597). - **RI-139** (pacing follow-ups): 2026-10-27 (merge of d3c5902 + review fix 55a083f). (c) each call's clock, size, attempt and call number live on its own `_Request`, never on the engine, and CostMeter takes the call number, so with `range_workers>1` no row carries another thread's call (a two-thread test fails on the old engine); range_labels reads each page's deployment off its own reply; the Foundry engine's cap lessons go on the call's own list. (a) a pacer hold before a request that never went out is a `not_sent` row counted in `paced_seconds`, not in calls (the harness's call_health skips it). (b) `paced_seconds` in earlier_attempts, all_attempts, results.json totals and the RESULTS failed-requests line, only when nonzero. (d) a retry-after of 0 takes the engine's ladder step (`RATE_LIMIT_LADDER`) for the retry and the next request's hold, never 0 and not the 60 s minute (that is for a rate limit naming no wait). (e) `is_rate_limit` reads a 429 status, the class name, or rate-limit words in the error's own words only (the message before an embedded body, the body's message/code/type), so an error echoing request text is not a rate limit; the Foundry 400 wording still is. (f) a running per-minute token count is not built (README).

- **RI-127** (pace after a large request): 2026-10-27 (merge of 606ee2c, reviewed). `score_on_cluster(tokens_per_minute=)` / `Budgets.tokens_per_minute` (default None, nothing paced or sent differently): after a request estimated above half the budget comes back, the next request to that model waits out 60 s; a rate limit with no retry-after (including Foundry's 400-as-rate-limit, filed by `engine.error_kind` from the glue's RateLimitGaveUp wording) holds the model's next request a window; the Prompter ladder's waits are at least 60 s (worst case 540 s a request, 6 retries as before); `paced` on rows, `paced_seconds` in the cost block. - **OWN-99** (ask the AI FDE for the models' rate limits): 2026-10-27, answered by the FDE: the AI FDE cannot read Foundry's configured limits (its tools do not show them and the proxy's errors do not state them): they are set per model, in tokens and requests per minute, by the Foundry platform administrators in the Resource Management app (AIP usage and limits); as the FDE understands it they are one pool across the Foundry instance, which admins can split by project or kind of work, so our transforms likely share it with other users and jobs (possibly the published Streamlit app's Claude calls); it knows of no per-request input cap apart from the context window (a request near the minute's budget passes only when the minute is almost unused); the proxy sometimes reports a rate limit as 400 Bad Request, not 429 (run 5's give-ups already read as rate limits: 'RateLimitGaveUp: ... 400 Client Error'); its throttle at 22.5 GPT-5.4 calls a minute instead of 45 gave the resume run 16 rate-limit retries and no give-ups. The FDE also said Haiku 5.5 is not imported into the workspace (only Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Sol); the owner corrected this the same day: Haiku 5.5 is available on Foundry as `ri.language-model-service..language-model.anthropic-claude-5-5-haiku` (the owner's example transform declares it as a model input), so it most likely needs adding to the wave's transform as a model input, and the brief keeps Haiku describing (D2). The FDE offers a probe transform (a few small calls, recording any rate-limit headers the proxy returns) and a table of run 5's rate-limit events against request sizes (no model calls). Next: OWN-100 (the admin's numbers, Haiku's model input, the FDE's two offers); the brief's stop point 7 and D2 rewritten.

- **RI-137** (the calc reader's unit token lacked superscripts and micro): 2026-10-27 (merge of 2aacfee; lead read the diff). `calc_reader._UNIT_TOKEN` accepts ², ³, µ (U+00B5) and μ (U+03BC) inside a unit, and mu may open one (the unit table has µg/kg, µS/cm); a ² or ³ never opens a unit, so a footnote marker after a number is not read as one. The unit table already folds m² = m^2 = m2. µm/s is read with its unit but has no SI conversion in the table.

- **INF-53** (the app installs and runs on Foundry's numpy 1.26): 2026-10-26 (squash 74c9cc3, reviewed twice). Cause: the platform pins numpy <2 through pyspark, and pip put scipy 1.18.1 (numpy >=2) on top, whose own np.long broke scipy's optimize, interpolate, integrate and stats; 'SALib is not installed' was pandas 3 failing to import. `constraints/foundry-numpy126.txt` (numpy 1.26.4, scipy <=1.17.1, pandas <3 for SALib 1.5.1, contourpy, opencv-python-headless, SALib, liquepy, pygef at their newest numpy-1.26 releases) with `constraints/foundry_requirements.py`; `docs/FOUNDRY.md` §1 item 4. np.trapezoid through `geotech_common/numpy_compat.py` (15 sites; a guard test scans for names numpy 1.26 lacks or 2.x removed). PyNiteFEA has no numpy-1.26 release: pynite is listed only where present (find_spec, no import at load), the structural specialist is told when it is absent, salib offers only modules present. numpy floor >=1.26 (a fresh resolve without the constraints still gives numpy 2). CI job 'numpy 1.26 (Foundry)'. Checked with no model calls: 16 of the 17 failed questions identified, their 20 tool calls run with answer-key values in tolerance; the July eval's 106 tool calls replay with 0 numpy errors (was 25 in 15 questions), numbers as on numpy 2. planlens floor lowered on its branch (6ae6441). The eval itself reruns on Foundry with the next wheel (INF-40).

B sent 50-56 pages to the wrong reader on the 13 stage-b reports against 182 and 290 (in sample). Carried out in RI-104.
- **EV-08** (the geotech eval in a numpy-2 Foundry repo): 2026-10-26, answered by the AI FDE's check (report only): no Foundry transforms repo can have numpy 2, because the platform's transforms package pins a pyspark that requires numpy <2. The eval therefore runs on numpy 1.26: INF-53 makes the app install and run consistently there, then reruns the eval (owner, desk 2026-10-26: 'resolved with the FDE reply').

- **RI-95** (turn the report-library sub-agent on in the app): 2026-10-26 (squash 2430de1, lead read the diff): the app had no setting for a shared library, so one was added. `GEOTECH_REPORT_LIBRARY` names a shared library folder: the ingest writes each report to `<library>/<file name>-<8 hex of the file's hash>` (the record key's own hash, `writers.source_digest`, so the same file resumes in its own folder and two PDFs sharing a name get two), and the geotech page's report_library sub-agent reads it. Unset, the library is the conversation's own `report_ingest` folder. `library_available` keeps the tool and its prompt line off until a record exists; never on the Document Review page or the reviewer specialists. Where the folder lives is the deployment's choice (the Foundry project, as with OWN-97). The library is checked when the agent is built, so a conversation's first ingest shows up on the next build.

- **INF-49** (pystra 2.0): 2026-10-23 pystra 2.0 replaced the whole analysis API, not only `Transformation.jacobian` (now `jacobian_u_wrt_x`): `Form`/`Sorm`/`AnalysisOptions`/`CrudeMonteCarlo(options, ...)` became `FORM`/`SORM`/`FORMOptions`/`SimulationOptions`/`CrudeMonteCarlo(sm, lsf, options=, rng=)` returning result objects, and `addVariable`/`setCorrelation` became `add_variable`/`set_correlation` (read from the installed 2.0.0 package, `pystra/migrate/_data.py`). `pystra_agent` now runs FORM, SORM and Monte Carlo through one helper (`pystra_utils.run_form`, `run_monte_carlo`) on either version; the `<2` cap is lifted. On a mixed lognormal/Gumbel/constant/normal problem FORM beta, pf, alpha and design point agree between 1.6.0 and 2.0.0 to about 1e-15, SORM Breitung beta to about 2e-9; Monte Carlo draws differ between versions (2.x ignores numpy's global generator; it is seeded from it so `np.random.seed` still repeats a run). Two fixes of old faults: a uniform variable was built as `Uniform(name, a, b)`, which pystra reads as mean a and std b on both versions (a uniform on [2, 6] had mean 2), now built on its bounds; SORM mapped alpha and the design point onto all variables including constants, now onto the random ones as FORM did. 363 tests pass under 1.6.0 and under 2.0.0. foundry/ and foundry_test_harness/ use none of the removed names. Commits `070bf7f`, `f656d5b`, merge `9f1b394`.

- **RI-17** (calc reader): 2026-10-23 (1) the merge keeps a printed value by its PLACE, not by its label alone: a slot is a label at one place (same page, boxes on one printed line), so two values printed under one label (two load cases, two footings) are both kept, and same-place pairs settle first so the order the floor found them in cannot drop one; at the same place `floor.has_evidence` decides as before. A check that the page prints the reader's value inside its box was tried and removed in review (no truth gain, 108/145 with or without it; by hand most of its flips made a right reading lose). (2) A printout stays one item: `split_calc_runs` cuts only where a page's title names a different calculation (a whole word of the kind vocabulary, on a line set as a title: bold where the page is not, or 15 % larger than its body, alone on its line, not columns, not a unit-bracket axis label), where the program changes, or where a banner returns; runs over 22 pages (`MAX_RUN_PAGES`, what two calls show) are cut into balanced parts. A run longer than one 12-page window always takes its second call, and the two answers fold by place (a value is replaced only by the same printed line read again; both calls' unsettled lists kept); the continuation asks only for the pages it shows. - **INF-17** (PyMuPDF 2.0 breaks the table reads): 2026-10-19, cause: in 2.0 `import pymupdf` loads pymupdf4llm, which turns the quad corrections off for the whole process (glyph-tight boxes split "A-1" into "A1\n-"), and the ONNX layout model, which `find_tables`' default `use_layout=True` uses to merge side-by-side tables. Fix in planlens main (0fae05d, review 1e8e328, pushed, unreleased): `planlens/pymupdf_settings.py` turns the corrections on for each read under a process-wide lock (two reads at once on 1.28/2.0 had left small glyph heights on and stdout redirected for the rest of the process; that race is in the released 0.13) and calls `find_tables(use_layout=False)`; floor PyMuPDF 1.27.2 (1.23 fails 87 tests). App: `markup_check` reads its word boxes through it (00b130b); floor `PyMuPDF>=1.27.2,<2`. Suites pass on 1.27.2.3, 1.28.2 and 2.0 (planlens 1689/1717, report_ingest 1401, markup 115). Lifting the app's cap -> INF-18. Commit `00b130b`.

- **GM-142** (module defaults that are not stated): built 2026-10-18 by a helper on branch cloud/gm142-gm143, reviewed and merged. Each default is now correct or stated in `choices` with its basis and alternatives: settlement's secondary compression uses each layer's own e0 (Cα/(1+e0)); a strip with no L is a strip (L/B large; Schmertmann plane strain), the elastic method refuses one without Iw, a rectangle with no L is refused, a square or circle takes L = B; the consolidation tool needs q_net and B when no delta_sigma is given; a drilled shaft's cohesionless tip with no N60 is refused (it was a silent 15); an aggregate pier's n is stated with GEC-13's printed ranges (2.0-5.0 stone columns, Sec 4.1.2.4; 4-45 under rigid footings and 5-10 under embankments for rammed aggregate piers, p. 5-45) and E_column is no longer printed as an input; the seismic wall's static KA stays Rankine with Coulomb (KAE's delta, i, beta at kh = 0) stated as the alternative. Release notes: secondary compression at e0 0.9 20.83 -> 21.92 mm; a strip with no L, 2:1 stress at q 150 kPa, B 2 m, z 4 m 16.7 -> 50 kPa (consolidation 56 -> 139 mm; the elastic case now refused); the library's SettlementAnalysis with no L takes L = B (was 1.0 m); a shaft with a cohesionless tip and no N60 is refused; WE-SHAFT-1 gives the bearing sand its printed N60 = 41 (GEC-10 App. A p. A-11); eval DS-2 states its tip N60 = 15. One review (it caught three GEC-13 statements the manual does not print). Commits `0814d9f`, `127a7ed`. Follow-ups GM-148, GM-149, GM-150.
- **GM-109** (`plot_data` cannot place free text): built 2026-10-18 as one general option. `plot_data` takes `annotations: [{text, x, y, coords: "data"|"axes", point: [x, y], align, box, color, font_size}]`: a note at a place in data units or as fractions of the plot, an optional leader arrow to a point, on every mode including plan views, at the same place in the PNG and the interactive version; point names keep clear of notes; bad keys get a did-you-mean (ha -> align, xy -> point, bbox -> box), a clamped font size warns. A call without notes draws byte-for-byte what it did. Notes are checked by the ONE layout rule GM-108 built, now shared as `geotech_common/layout_ink.py` (the plotting box loads the same code) and safe in the app's threads (a lock, other threads pass straight through); leaders count as lines, so a leader crossing a note is reported in plot_code too. Measured on 240 one-note figures across 8 modes: 50 reports, 0 false (the first draft's box check had 21 false of 37). Two w9 figures that went to plot_code only for their text (a plasticity chart, Mohr circles) now draw through plot_data. One review. Follow-ups GM-146, GM-147. Commit `c186589`.
- **GM-108** (`layout_check` judged by drawn pixels): built 2026-10-18 as one rule for every kind of drawn thing, not the w9 cases one by one. The check redraws each piece of the figure alone in the saved frame and draw order: a line, marker, tick, outline, mesh or shape edge collides with a label only where it shows on the label's letters (rotation included); an opaque box or legend frame hides what is under it, and a label's own box is part of its footprint; legend entries are checked as text; text outside the axes is checked; a new `text_cut_off` names text cut at the edge of its axes or of the figure; 3D text is measured where it is drawn. Bounded: past 256 MB or 3 s the check says "unchecked" instead of slowing or failing the figure. The report keeps its shape plus `box.layout_check_seconds` (0.09-0.31 s on the w9 figures). Measured on the 13 rebuilt w9 figures against the wave review's verdicts: false reports 22 of 36 -> 1 of 16 (a dashed line visibly through an 80 %-white box, kept), missed collisions 1 -> 0. One review (24 adversarial figures; it caught memory on many-line figures, 3D text, meshes, collection edges and an unbounded time). Follow-up GM-145. Commit `f9307c6`.
- **REF-26** (reference tool results the app's 16,000-character cap cut mid-JSON): built 2026-10-18 for every reference-facing tool, not the three listings named. The library's list_chapters, retrieve_section, search_sections, load_chapter, reference_search, reference_query, figure_search and figure_get take an optional size limit (offset paging on the searches, `chapter=` on list_chapters); an over-long result comes back with fewer hits, shorter snippets or a chapter outline and a note saying how to get the rest. The adapters shape every result to 14,000 characters and pass the new arguments only to a library that takes them. Results over the cap across every reference and worst-case argument: 371 -> 0 (largest search_sections 708,876 -> 13,610, load_chapter 206,140 -> 13,795, reference_query 258,786 -> 13,463, list_chapters 27,820 -> 7,749). list_chapters without a chapter still lists every section where that fits (22 of 27 references). Paging past 500 is an error, never a repeated page; a row too wide asks for fewer columns; plain retrieve_section names the nearest sections on a missing number. A guard test (`funhouse_agent/tests/test_reference_result_sizes.py`) walks every reference method over every reference and fails on any result over the cap or not valid JSON, or on a new method it does not walk. One review (it caught the offset clamp at 500 that made the agent loop, a wrong OFFSET hint, an over-wide row and the guard's blind spot). The v1 agent (agent.py) still cuts call_agent at 8,000 (historical path). Refs commit `db84be2`. App commit `582437e`. Follow-up REF-27.
- **REF-24** (the librarian sees only top-level sections): built 2026-10-18 for every reference, not GEC-12 alone. One section tree serves the plain retrieval and the search database: GEC-12's 76 nested subsections and the flat-listed deeper sections of every other reference sit under their container. The database holds one row per section at every level with its own text only (3,884 rows), so a search finds the section that holds the words ("Nordlund" -> GEC-12 7.2.1.3.1, not 7.2); hits say where they sit (`parent_id`, `under`); a fetch works at any level and lists its `children`; a parent no longer inlines its subtree (GEC-12 7.2 was 28,490 characters and the app's 16,000 cap cut it mid-JSON, so 7.2.2-7.2.10 were never seen), with `include_subsections` inlining only within 12,000. An older database rebuilds itself; the app's adapters pass the new option only to a library that has it. Measured: 14 questions answered in a subsection, recall@5 7/14 -> 14/14, each reached in one search and one fetch of 1-7k characters (GEC-12's were ~18k cut fetches); 3 of 2,260 top-level title hits moved; the cross-reference eval 4/9 -> 3/8 because GEC-12's own Downdrag and liquefaction subsections now rank first (on topic; top-1 disturbance 0/10). One review. Refs commit `d5f7f52`. App commit `28ae54f`. Follow-ups REF-26, REF-25.
- **REF-23** (GEC-12 chapter 7 section numbers differ from the printed manual): built 2026-10-18 as one check over every reference rather than the one chapter. `geotech-references/scripts/check_section_numbers.py` compares every chapter file's section numbers and titles with the source PDF's outline and page headings and renumbers only a section it can place with certainty (`--detail` lists the rest); `tests/test_section_numbers.py` runs it per reference (the PDFs are in the repo, so CI runs it too). 39 renumbered from the print: GEC-12 ch 7 (the static methods under 7.2.1.3, rock 7.2.1.4, software 7.2.1.5), micropile ch 3-5 (19), GEC-7 ch 3-4 (4), GEC-10 ch 3/14 (3), the pavements manual ch 4/7 (2), GEC-13 ch 2 (1); three in-text cross-references follow. Two wrong GEC-13 citations in `ground_improvement/calc_steps.py` corrected. One review: all 39 checked against the printed pages. Refs commit `4dc357f`. App commit `7157faf`. Follow-ups REF-24 (the librarian sees only top-level sections), REF-25 (what the check could not settle).
- **GM-25** (Nordlund chart fits): superseded by GM-121, which reads Figs. 7-9, 7-14 and 7-16 and Tables 7-6/7-7 as printed. Commit `079fed0`.
- **GM-121** (two GEC-12 limits the driven-pile module did not apply: the toe's effective stress and an open pipe's unplugged case): built 2026-10-18 as an audit of the whole of GEC-12 chapter 7 against the module, step by step, read off the printed manual. Nordlund: delta/phi from Fig. 7-9 by V (was a fixed 0.75 / 0.90), K-delta from Tables 7-6/7-7 log-linear in V, CF from Fig. 7-14, alpha-t and N'q from Fig. 7-16 (alpha-t was 1), Step 8's 3 ksf limit on the toe's effective stress (p. 243), the H-pile toe on the box area with steel as an option, p. 232's width limits as warnings. Tomlinson alpha: Fig. 7-17 by su, pile type and D/b as printed (traced to about 0.02 ksf; H-piles on the corrugated curve per p. 247), Ca capped at su (API alpha <= 1.0, p. 252), and above the chart Ca held at the chart's end as GEC-12's own worked example does (API RP 2A an option). Beta: a clay toe Nt 3 (p. 256; was about 22). Open pipes: inside side resistance 1/3 of outside (an input), the plug weight Wp subtracted (Eq. 7-89), the sand toe limit 105 ksf, Eq. 7-90 for pipes under 24 in in clay. Uplift: the full nominal shaft resistance (Eq. 7-38; the reduction is in phi-up). Each is stated in `choices` with its basis and alternatives; the judgment calls are OWN-63. Numbers (release note, kN): a deep sand H-pile 5834.5 -> 5717.2 (uplift 4015 -> 5236); a closed pipe in sand 3175.1 -> 3269.7; an open pipe in sand 3175.1 -> 2987.7 (plugged governs; unplugged 4455.1 -> 3119.6); a concrete pile in clay 1326.8 -> 2043.2 and a closed pipe in clay 1015.6 -> 1610.6 (the printed Fig. 7-17 in place of one simplified curve); a layered H-pile 2763.6 -> 2790.3; beta with a clay toe 1222.7 -> 753.7; V-001's shaft within 1.2 % of published (its toe 16 % lower: the example did not apply Step 8); V-002 +13 to +14 %; WE-SF-2's shaft +0.5 % of published; WE-PILE-1 3480.1 -> 3147.2. One review (it caught the uplift under Eq. 7-90 taken from the unreduced shaft, and changed the two defaults above to the conservative printed readings). Supersedes GM-25. Follow-ups OWN-63, GM-144. Commit `079fed0`.
- **GM-141** (calc packages re-implemented their tools, so they drifted: a wave-equation pile given in Pa 1000x too stiff, a pile group's layout dropped, a different fill unit weight, defaults the tools require): built 2026-10-18 as the structural fix. One mechanism in `funhouse_agent/adapters/calc_package.py` (`_PACKAGE_TOOLS`, `_run_tool`, `_package_parameters`): every package takes its inputs as its tool does (names, older names, required inputs and defaults from the tool's own adapter and METHOD_INFO), runs the tool's own code path (`run_<tool>`, split out of 13 adapters), and prints the inputs, coefficients and `defaults_used` the run used; its parameter docs come from the tool's, so the unit and name drift cannot recur. All 13 packages migrated; the MSE package refuses as before and points to `mse_wall`; the slope packages wait for GM-140. Tool results are unchanged (49 of 54 calls byte-identical; the rest are `axial_pile_capacity` refusing a call with no pile_type as its docs said, `seismic_earth_pressure` taking KA, `downdrag_analysis` passing `allowable_settlement`, and `combined_settlement` returning each consolidation layer's description). Package numbers that move (release note): the pile-group efficiency is computed when the layout is given (it said "not computed"; 0.7788 on the test case), with the smaller of the two spacings stated; a downdrag package without the fill unit weight takes the tool's 19 (was 20); a settlement package without B, or an axial one without pile_type, is refused as the tool is. Calc steps render from a partial analysis dict (the library path) on the modules' own defaults. A parity test checks every package against its tool and, on 21 cases, against the analysis module called directly, and one number read off the HTML. One review. Follow-ups GM-143, notes on GM-113, GM-114, GM-132, GM-142. Commit `c9752a0`.
- **GM-120** (calc packages used a fixed constant when an input was left out although inputs the call gave determine it; the seismic package printed Rankine Ka from phi but computed PA with 0.333): built 2026-10-18 as the class, each by the same function the analysis tool uses. Seismic package, KA not given: KA is Rankine from phi via the tool's own `rankine_Ka`, stated in `choices` (module's choice; KA given is the alternative), and the PA step, summary row and figure print the Ka the run used whether worked out or given. Settlement package, L not given: L = B as the tool does (it was 1.0 m whatever B). Ground-improvement package: inputs by an older name (E_pier) are printed as given, and the tool's own names (`stress_concentration_ratio`, `column_modulus`) now reach the package (they were silently dropped). Numbers (release note): seismic at phi 34 PA 113.89 -> 96.69 kN/m, delta-PAE 7.43 -> 24.63, PAE unchanged 121.32; settlement at B 3 m total 39.42 -> 87.00 mm (matches the tool); a package given KA now prints that KA (results unchanged). Packages given every input are byte-identical to HEAD. One review. The structural cause, packages re-implementing their tools, is GM-141; module-level defaults GM-142. Commit `6d8a7c5`.
- **GM-128** (a circle tangent to the model's base was accepted or refused depending on the slice count): built 2026-10-18. After GM-134 it no longer depended on the count, but the tolerance in force was an accident of the slicing, under a micron, so a published circle recovered 3.5 mm under the base was refused. Now the depth below the deepest layer's bottom is measured on the surface itself (`slices.base_depth`, exact for circles and polylines, and for a base with vertical steps), not on its slices: a given surface within 1/1000 of the model's height of the base touches it and keeps every slice at any count; deeper is refused at any count, with its depth and x. Searches stay inside the model (1e-6 of its height), so the radius search closes on the tangent circle from above. Taylor's tangent circle is pinned at 25-80 slices; circles 5-10 cm under, and well under either side of a step in the base, are refused. Every recorded result moved by under 0.0001 and none rose. One review (it caught a step in the base read on the wrong side; fixed). The last slope fix before the integral review: further findings are in GM-140. Commit `4f2b05a`.
- **GM-138** (the default Spencer / M-P / GLE circular search missed the critical circle on V-043, 1.2375 against 1.186): built 2026-10-18. For the rigorous methods without supports, the walk that refines the grid's best centres moved on Bishop's FOS; on V-043 it went to shallow circles on the sand face that Bishop ranks lowest (near the infinite-slope value) and Spencer does not, so the search kept a grid centre. Now Bishop still picks each centre's radius but the method itself scores that circle, so the walk moves only where the method's own FOS falls; the method's radius is re-optimised at the end (through the same m_alpha and ground-crossing checks). General: no case-specific seed or range. Every move is down: V-043 Spencer 1.2375 -> 1.1859 (brute force 1.1858; 1.190 on 480 slices), M-P / GLE 1.2464 -> 1.1958, V-031 1.4177 -> 1.4174, V-042 and Duncan Ex 4 by 0.0003; Bishop searches, ACADS, #85, V-054 and Taylor unchanged; each default search within 0.0013 of its best-known circle. V-043's critical circle is tangent to the soft clay's base and sits 1.6 % below Slide2's circular 1.209: the inputs match the source (dry, no crack in the sand fill), Slide2's own noncircular search finds 1.188 and 56 kN/m on the same slide (ours 57 kN/m), and its circular 1.209 and 44-45 kN/m match the circle the tests used to pin (1.2096, 41 kN/m), so a gridded circular search stopped short of a circle tangent to the model's base. Spencer searches take 0-1.5 s longer. One review. Follow-ups GM-139 and a note on GM-117. Commit `04f4759`.
- **GM-134** (circles were cut evenly, so a slice across a layer or water-table crossing took one material, and each slice took the tangent at its midpoint, which falls short where the arc turns vertical; V-043's circle read 1.246-1.180 between 25 and 31 slices): built 2026-10-18. Circles get a slice boundary at each ground break and at each layer, water-table and tension-crack-base crossing and surcharge edge (polylines also gain surcharge and crack edges), and every slice takes its base angle and length from the chord between its boundaries (the straight bases Duncan, Wright & Brandon assume; Slide2 takes the moment arm at the base mid-point and slices at piezometric crossings); the old midpoint tangent's error fell only as n^-1/2 (a vertical cut 0.8005 / 0.8220 / 0.8325 / 0.8393 at 30 / 120 / 480 / 3,840 slices), the chord's falls as 1/n (0.8416 / 0.8427). Every moved value lands on the old code's own fine-sliced value (66 of 69 surfaces toward it, median error 1.76 % -> 0.09 %): V-043 Spencer 1.180 -> 1.210 (Slide2 1.209; the force to reach 1.35 now 40.9 kN/m against 44-45); V-031 1.411 / 1.415 -> 1.421 / 1.425 (1.417 / 1.422); V-026 1.497 -> 1.485; V-045 3.445 -> 3.482; the vertical cut 0.800 -> 0.842 (Taylor 0.851); Slide2 #85 Bishop 1.304 -> 1.372, and with reverse curvature discarded 1.511 / 1.316 (D&W 1.51 / 1.32); V-054 is now checked on its governing base circle, 2.221 (published 2.16, FLAC 2.18). A FOS no longer depends on the slice count (V-043 spread over 25-45 slices 0.066 -> 0.003). Rigorous searches on phi = 0 slopes take 18-25 % longer (GM-137). One review. Follow-ups GM-135 (pond edge), GM-137, GM-138. Commit `553aa58`.
- **GM-133** (the CPT liquefaction route used a 1.0 m water table, and a 0.25 g PGA, that no one gave and nothing showed): built 2026-10-17. They came from our own wrapper's defaults (`liquepy_agent.analyze_cpt_liquefaction(gwl=1.0, pga=0.25)`, the unified tool's undeclared `_first(..., default=1.0)`, `liquepy.cpt_liquefaction` and `analyze_field_correlations`); liquepy itself takes the water table as a required input. Every liquefaction route now REQUIRES the water table and the PGA, as the SPT routes always did, and refuses a call without them with the reason: the water table sets sigma'v in CSR = 0.65 (sigma_v / sigma'v) amax rd (Youd et al. 2001; Boulanger & Idriss 2014), and GEC 5 has the site investigation establish the groundwater level. A CPT result's `choices` names the water table it used, given; the CRR curve's C0 = 2.8 is stated as the module's choice. No worked example or calc package relied on the 1.0 m; 11 calls are identical to HEAD with the water table given (the calc package byte for byte). Follow-up note on GM-132. Commit `6319836`.
- **GM-131** (a noncircular surface was sliced at an even spacing and each slice took the base angle at its midpoint, so a slice across a corner took one segment's angle for its whole weight; one Taylor D = 2 surface read 0.650-1.199 between 29 and 45 slices, and a fixed polyline could err either way by about 22 %): built 2026-10-17. A polyline gets a slice boundary at each corner, each ground break and each crossing of a layer boundary or the water table (Slide2 slices at the piezometric-surface crossings; Duncan, Wright & Brandon ch. 6); the stretches between share the slices by width, and each slice takes its base angle and length from its chord; a corner is never merged away except within 1e-6 m of the entry or exit. Circles are unchanged (GM-134). The planar family's surfaces sit 1/20 of a slice width deep and its ramps are their own slices; the figure draws each slice line from the surface. That surface now reads 0.970-0.973 at 29-45 slices; Taylor D = 2 noncircular 0.610 -> 0.680 (circle 0.706). Pinned values move to where the old code converges at very fine slicing (checked independently): V-028 0.792 -> 0.808 (referee 0.78); V-030 1.190 -> 1.218 (Slide2 1.157, Duncan 1.17; the rest of the gap is GM-136); Fredlund & Krahn B2 Spencer 1.354 -> 1.386 (1.373, Slide2 1.382; its other values up 2-3 %); V-043 differential evolution 1.09 -> 1.234 (Slide2 1.188; a search shortfall, a strict xfail under GM-117); V-041 Corps search 0.895 on a circle -> 0.783 on the drained infinite slope (closed form 0.772); WE-SLOPE-5 1.233 -> 1.344 (closed form 1.343). Two reviews. Follow-ups GM-134, GM-135, GM-136. Commit `ca8f08f`.
- **GM-129** (slope wording: "centre grid" on entry/exit results; no cost for differential evolution or weak-layer searches): built 2026-10-17 with GM-118. Entry/exit warnings say "entry/exit pairs"; the `method` description gives measured times for noncircular (6-7 s), weak-layer (3-7 s) and differential-evolution (10-12 s; 41 s on a live-run slope) searches at 30 slices. No numbers change in the batch (64 `call_agent` calls identical to HEAD once `choices`, `defaults_used` and warnings are set aside). One review. Follow-ups GM-131 (found by the build: a noncircular surface's slices straddle its corners), GM-132, GM-133. Commit `92f9934`.
- **GM-125** (the agent never quoted `choices`, and slope's choice named families that were not scored): built 2026-10-17 with GM-118. The slope entry is "surface families scored": only the families that gave an admissible surface, and why each other family it ran gave none (equal to the `candidates` keys on every search type); `governing_location` and `refused_lowest` are pointed at only when present; `choices` is named in the returns of the nine choice-bearing methods and both slope searches, and `call_agent`'s descriptions name `choices` and `defaults_used`. Commit `92f9934`.
- **GM-119** (`defaults_used` listed defaults the run never used): built 2026-10-17 with GM-118. Parameters carry `applies_when` (other parameters' values, set or unset, any-of lists, or false), declared with `adapters.declare_applies_when`, which fails on import if a name is wrong; `describe_method` hides it from the model. Tagged: liquefaction, lateral and axial piles, settlement, the seismic, settlement and slope-report packages, and the slope tools; the paths were traced in review (settlement's stress method only with consolidation layers, axial `cohesive_phi` only under beta, `gamma_w` only on the NCEER route, the slope grid settings by surface type). Worst-case worked example 2,961 -> 2,800 characters; 64 calls 44,735 -> 41,321. Follow-up GM-132. Commit `92f9934`.
- **GM-118** (a value typed equal to its default was labelled "default" in `choices`, while `defaults_used` left it out): built 2026-10-17 with GM-119, GM-125 and GM-129. `geotech_common/choices.call_inputs()` is a ContextVar that `call_agent` sets with the names a call gave and the declared defaults it left in force (token reset in a finally; nested calls get their own record; safe across threads and asyncio, checked with 300 mixed calls); `how_given(..., name=)` reads it first at 11 sites, nested values are still compared by value, and a direct module call falls back to the value comparison; a name reported as "default" is always listed in `defaults_used` when the method declares it. Commit `92f9934`.
- **GM-127** (only GM-122's new circle family applied Slide2's m_alpha < 0.2 check; Bishop, Janbu and the noncircular searches accepted such surfaces, e.g. Duncan Ex 4 with Janbu read 1.076 on a circle with m_alpha 0.007, and a live run reported a differential-evolution surface at 1.57 with m_alpha -0.37): built 2026-10-17. One shared rule (Whitman & Bailey 1967; Slide2 error -112): a surface fails when m_alpha = cos a + sin a tan phi'/F < 0.2 on a slice with phi' > 0, checked at each method's own F (Bishop; Janbu uncorrected; Spencer, M-P and GLE by the GLE normal-force denominator, Fredlund & Krahn 1977; the ordinary method has none and says so). Slices with phi' = 0 are exempt, because there m_alpha = cos a does not change the FOS (c b / m_alpha = c l): checking them left out real crack-closed toe circles (a vertical 5 m cut in undrained clay went 0.800 -> 0.933 against Taylor's 0.851 in the first build; caught by review); Slide2's every-slice rule is the option `m_alpha_rule='every_slice'`, named in `choices`. Every search family leaves such surfaces out and counts them (`n_rejected_m_alpha`, summary, calc row, `choices`, a warning when nothing holds); a single surface the user gives is reported with a warning, as Slide2 allows; the governing surface is solved again at twice the slices and a warning fires only when its FOS moves more than 1 %; differential evolution scores a refused surface by how far m_alpha falls short (it ran its full 2,325 evaluations) and falls back to the seeding search's best admissible surface. Numbers against HEAD: Duncan Ex 4 Janbu 30 slices 1.076 -> 1.199 (1.213 at 120); Slide2 #85 with reverse curvature discarded 1.467 -> 1.432 active and 1.296 -> 1.274 passive (1.484 / 1.307 at 240 slices; D&W 1.51 / 1.32), the safe side, because the circle-above-toe family now uses the shared rule; every other pinned value unchanged (the vertical cut pinned at 0.800 against Taylor 0.851); w11's differential-evolution 1.570 (m_alpha -0.37) -> 6.85 on an admissible sandstone surface (its coverage: GM-117). VALIDATION.md's #85 Spencer / GLE rows corrected (1.542 / 1.584). Two reviews. Follow-up GM-130. Commit `654b144`.
- **GM-123** (the slope search had no time or trial budget: a Spencer 30x30 entry/exit grid at 60 slices took 957 s live, a second ran past the turn's limit and the user got no answer): built 2026-10-17. `time_budget_s` on every search entry point; the library's default is None (no budget, so pinned results never depend on machine speed), and the agent's tools (the slope tool, its Monte Carlo re-searches, the calc package, the project run) give each search 180 s (`SEARCH_TIME_BUDGET_S`; the agent may give up to 600 s, more is refused). Grids are scored coarse, then refined around the best few points before the next level, so a cut search has spanned the range and found the valley (on the w10 Bishop map within 1 % of the minimum after 44 pairs, against 713 coarse-first); noncircular, PSO and DE stop between trials, iterations or generations and keep the best surface; a cut search opens its warnings with "Cut short by its time budget: the reported FOS may be too high (unconservative)", what was scored of how many, the projected full time and the levers, and `search_ranges['time_budget']` and `choices` say the same; Monte Carlo counts the re-searches cut. The `method` description gives the cost per surface (Spencer, M-P, GLE about 30-70 times Bishop). `bishop_screen` (off by default; no reference prescribes it) scans circles with Bishop and re-scores the best with the rigorous method: same governing surface and FOS on ACADS, V-031, Taylor and w10 in 15-60 % of the time; not applied to noncircular surfaces or with supports, and `choices` says so. w10 Spencer 30x30 at 60 slices: 972 s -> 149 s cut at 154 of 900 pairs with the main minimum 0.9427 (the full run's), governing 0.9356; with the screen 33 s, full coverage. A search that finishes is identical to HEAD (ACADS, Taylor, V-031, Bishop and Spencer). Two reviews. Follow-up GM-128. Commit `8e8909e`.
- **GM-124** (reversed entry/exit windows returned nothing, silently): built 2026-10-17 with GM-122. An entry window given wholly right of the exit window is swapped and the warnings say so; a window given high bound first is read low bound first; an entry/exit search that scored nothing says so; the parameter descriptions say entry is the low-x end. Commit `123cfab`.
- **GM-122** (a slip circle below the ground over more than one stretch was scored from its first crossing to its last, across the air): built 2026-10-17. Such a circle or polyline is refused (`GroundCrossingError`, a ValueError) and counted in `n_rejected_ground_crossings`, as Slide2 does (exactly two intersection points with the ground; its error -105) and as Duncan, Wright & Brandon ch. 6 treat the sliding mass (one free body); a point within 1e-6 m of the ground does not split a stretch, and a stretch shallower than 1 % of the deepest is a touch. The refusal showed that the default search had no circles coming out on the face above the toe (a 10 m cut with a weak clay over rock then read 6.35 through the rock), so a new family, circles passing above the toe (`circle_above_toe`, FHWA NHI-06-088 p. 6-38), starts at 0.1-0.9 of each face's height, refines, and re-scores the best three with the rigorous method (Bishop screens for Spencer, M-P, GLE and Janbu); it leaves out circles with m_alpha < 0.2 on a slice (Slide2 error -112) and reverse curvature, reads no layers, respects the caller's windows, and `search_ranges['faces_covered']`, `choices` and a warning say when it governs. Numbers: that cut 1.16 -> 0.936 (0.935 by a fine directed search); the weak-layer test 4.82 -> 0.884 (0.885); V-042's pinned circle 1.325 -> 1.336 (published 1.334 / 1.340; beta 2.244 -> 2.308); Slide2 #85 at 30 slices 1.485 -> 1.467 and 1.302 -> 1.296 (1.508 / 1.318 at 240 slices against D&W 1.51 / 1.32); V-052b 0.724 -> 0.722. A default Bishop search takes about 1 s longer. Two reviews (the second caught a false low Janbu FOS from steep crest entries, fixed by screening with Bishop). Follow-ups GM-126, GM-127. Commit `123cfab`.
- **GM-115** (every module result says what it chose; owner, 2026-10-16: "make sure the agent is communicating its engineering judgements to the user"): built 2026-10-16. One helper, `geotech_common/choices.py` (`choice`, `how_given`, `plain`), gives each result a `choices` list: what was chosen, given or default, the basis with its reference, and the alternatives. axial_pile: the method by depth and layer, the toe, an open pipe plugged or unplugged (inside friction taken equal to the outside, Wp not subtracted), δ/φ, and σ'v at the toe above GEC-12's 3 ksf limit; lateral_pile: p-y sources and J; settlement: stress method, elastic Iw, the drainage path only when cv is given, an assumed 1-year t1 now a result warning, e0 for secondary; seismic earth pressure: M-O, kv, δ, and where M-O has no solution the Seed & Whitman estimate said plainly and a result warning naming Coulomb's trial wedge, the Culmann method or a slope stability analysis (GEC-11 Vol I Sec. 7.1.1); liquefaction: rd and MSF per layer, K_sigma, the (N1)60cs cap; slope: method and search ranges. `call_agent` results carry `defaults_used` (blank and calc-header keys dropped); calc packages print a Method Choices table in plain words. GEC-12, GEC-6, GEC-11, NHI-06-088 and UFC 3-220-20 citations checked against the printed manuals (several were wrong). No numbers change (29 calls compared with HEAD, then 9 after the fixes). Two reviews. Follow-ups GM-118 (a typed value equal to the default), GM-119 (defaults that do not apply), GM-120 (the seismic package's KA from phi), GM-121 (two GEC-12 limits the axial module does not apply), REF-23 (the library's GEC-12 ch. 7 section numbers). Commit `ed08f35`.
- **GM-57** (the slope search's automatic ranges were fractions of the model width; the Taylor φ = 0 check): built 2026-10-16. `slope_stability/search_ranges.py` sets them from the slope itself: toe, crest and height H of each face group and the depth d to the model base; centre heights 0.1H to max(2H, d) (Janbu's centre charts, FHWA NHI-06-088 Figs 6-16/6-19); the reach of the deepest base-tangent circle beyond the toe and behind the crest (Taylor Fig 6-15 fits inside). No minimum surface length: a tiny circle on a c′ = 0 face governs at the infinite-slope value and is labelled so, in the drawdown search too ((γ′/γ)·tanφ′/tanβ). Results carry `search_ranges`, `governing_location` (ends, lowest point, depth below ground, the layers along the base, position against toe and crest in H) and `refused_lowest` (a lower surface the search refused because its solution held about one moment axis only or at one slice count, with a warning). Taylor: the 1.92 is the D = ∞ chart value; at the model's D = 2.25 the chart gives ≈ 1.945 and the search, on a profile long enough, 1.935 (the 2.04 was a profile too short). Fixed on the way: a rapid-drawdown Fellenius stage-3 normal that counted the pond's pressure on a slice base in full but its weight at cos²α (an automatic search gave DWW 0.052). Pinned FOS moved mostly toward the published values (VALIDATION.md B14). By the owner's ruling, nothing is hard-coded for a thin weak layer over rock: the automatic search reads 4.82 there against 0.885, and given windows it finds 0.889 (pinned); whether the agent notices is EV-25. Independently reviewed twice. Rest: GM-117. Commit `c804d47`.
- **GM-116** (the agent states its judgment calls; owner, 2026-10-16: "User's judgement is the one that matters"): built 2026-10-16 behind `GEOTECH_JUDGMENT_CALLS` (OFF; arm `judgment_calls`). When on, the main geotech prompt says the engineering judgment is the user's: make each call as an experienced engineer would and give the answer, then put every call in front of the user to accept or override; an answer that rests on one ends with a short "Judgment calls" list (what was chosen; why, with its reference, asking the references helper when a reference governs; the alternative set aside; what would change the answer, a number only from a tool run). The calc helper lists its own calls; the librarian returns a reference's several methods or values as cited options and says which fits the question. With the switch off every prompt, helper and tool description is byte-identical (pinned; checked independently over seven builds). The smoke harness gains `expect.answer_names`; flows F61 / F61j (off / on) put a cut with a thin weak layer over rock to the agent and check that the answer names its search choice and what would change it. Measurement: EV-26, EV-25. Commit `8c8f095`.
- **DR-32** (the app's side of misspelt keys in `annotate_document`): built 2026-10-16. A `remove=` entry with a key other than `id`/`text` is refused with a did-you-mean (`markup_id` → `id`) before anything is written; the model is shown planlens' own markup fields (read from the installed spec, `pdf_page` added, `additionalProperties: false`, descriptions kept only where they add something: kind, bbox, points_at, min_score, pdf_page), while calls are still accepted as planlens accepts them (`color`, `box`, `page_bbox`, a nested `anchor`; no route uses strict mode, checked through a real ToolNode); marks written with no comment are named in `empty_comments`; `min_score` without `fuzzy` says it was not used. Found and fixed: an `author` inside a markup replaced the app's signature (one recorded call signed 28 marks "AI draft review"); it is dropped with a note. The tool's shown text 5,319 → 6,976 characters, capped at 7,500 by a test. Replay of 594 recorded calls: no new refusals. Rest: DR-33. Commit `4cb262e`.
- **GM-112** (MSE walls with a sloping backfill): built 2026-10-16 per FHWA GEC-11 Vol I (FHWA-NHI-10-024) §4.4: Coulomb Kab with δ = β (Eqs 4-2/4-3, p. 4-15; a broken backslope with β = δ = I, the ground 2H behind the face, Fig 4-4), the thrust over h = H + L·tanβ capped at H + S (Eq 4-7, p. 4-20) inclined at β, the slope fill V2 over the reinforced zone, sliding/eccentricity/bearing (Eqs 4-10/4-11/4-13, 4-16, 4-21), internal Ka at β = 0 with the slope's equivalent surcharge (Eq 4-29), H1 and Zp below the slope; Kab from the retained fill (`phi_retained` / `gamma_retained` now passed through). Whether the traffic thrust's vertical part resists sliding is an option (`traffic_vertical_resists`, default no per p. 4-22; Example E1's choice available). Pinned against GEC-11 Vol II Examples E3 and E1 as V-056/V-057 (Kaf 0.537, sliding CDR 1.35/1.60/1.07, e 2.90/3.81 ft, σv 10.19/7.16/9.02 ksf; E1 Kab 0.360, CDR 1.12, σV-F 6.55 ksf). Still refused with a reason: a slope steeper than φ, a surcharge on a slope that continues. Also fixed (release note): traffic is left out of the pullout σv (p. 4-49, "neglecting traffic loads"; a 6 m wall with 12 kPa traffic, FOS_pullout at the top level 10.49 → 3.38), and strip F*/Kr reach their deep values at 20 ft = 6.096 m, not 6 m (E3 F* 1.411 → 1.420, published 1.420). Level-wall external stability unchanged (868 snapshots). Rest: GM-114. Commit `022a1b7`.
- **GM-110** (top-level parameter names never checked by many tools): built 2026-10-16. One check in dispatch (`_check_parameter_names`) refuses an unknown top-level name before the method runs, with a did-you-mean and the valid list, against METHOD_INFO: about 145 parameters the handlers read are now declared and 228 aliases declared (`declare_aliases`); afterwards no adapter's own check disagrees with METHOD_INFO. Reference functions are refused by their own signature, and those with a `**kwargs` catch-all are checked when the catch-all is never read (the seven DM7 plot functions; `figure_search`'s result-cap names declared as aliases). A method can list names it does not take, with a reason and the values that mean "not used": MSE `backfill_slope` (a level backfill only; 0 accepted; GM-112), `pile_group_simple` lateral loads (need `pile_group_6dof`; 0 accepted), `cantilever_excavation` `excavation_width` / `supports`, `braced_excavation` `excavation_width` (never read; 9 recorded calls had dropped it), `pile_group_package` `spacing`; the package refuses nonzero lateral loads without `method_6dof`. Before, `mse_wall` gave the same answer at 0° and 26.6° of backfill slope. Replays of 930 recorded calls: every refusal right. Follow-ups GM-112, GM-113, REF-22. Commit `09d39f9`.
- **GM-58** (nested dicts read with `.get`, so a misspelt key was dropped): built 2026-10-16. `check_nested_keys` / `check_nested_list` / `field_keys` now live in `funhouse_agent/adapters/__init__.py`; the nested items of 19 adapters refuse an unknown key with the item, the key, a did-you-mean and the valid keys, never renaming: layers in axial_pile, drilled_shaft, sheet_pile, soe, settlement, lateral_pile (per p-y model), fem2d (per analysis), pystrata, opensees, pavement and downdrag (and its `consolidation` layers, where a misspelt `C_ec` or `p_c` ran a layer as overconsolidated throughout); piles, supports, struts, phases, nodes, members, loads, series, annotations, site data and DXF mappings. Did-you-means never point at a different quantity (`cr` offers Cr or C_er, with a note). Calc packages read a layer as their analysis tool does (sheet_pile through the tool's own layer builder, so wall friction reaches it; axial_pile passes `toe_friction_angle`; drilled_shaft says it runs the depth beta method). Found and fixed: `dxf_import.build_slope_geometry` always raised AttributeError (its tests mocked it; now unmocked end to end), dxf_import wrote HS parameters nested where fem2d reads them flat, and a test fixture's `depth` was read as 0 m. An independent review replayed 187 recorded real calls: none broken. Top-level parameter names: GM-110. Commit `be134d9`.
- **GM-107** (wave w9's figure fixes, TRACE_REVIEW fixes 1-4, 7-9, 11-13, 15): built 2026-10-16. By default: a chat card shows the newer of a figure's PNG and its chart (a tie goes to the chart; a figure removed during the turn gets no card); `plot_data` keeps an axis range in the order given (log axes and the chart too) and takes `x_on_top`. Behind `GEOTECH_PLOT_CODE` / `GEOTECH_FIGURE_HELPER` (OFF; with both off the agent build is unchanged apart from plot_data's wording, pinned by `test_switches_off_leave_the_default_agent_unchanged`): `replaces=` retires drafts drawn this turn (working folder only, figure files only, after a successful draw, never older than the turn or the helper run); looks ask what the figure shows against the data before the lettering, and readings are joined by straight lines or points, never a stair; nothing the user did not give goes on a figure; the figure helper's own run stops at its third draw across tools and names; its result to the main agent lists what it drew and what each look found, and the main prompt names the helper; the nearest worked example goes with the brief (whole-word match, a one-word name needs two more words); lettering size at print width; the report PNG at the dpi asked with a look copy beside it. An independent review found four defects (a flaky card test, the cap reaching the calc helper, a wrong example match, no date guard outside a turn); all fixed. Leftovers: GM-111. Commit `e18293f`.
- **GM-49** (how deep below the toe settlement counts; the backlog's 10 %-of-effective-stress rule is GEC-12's, not UFC's): built 2026-10-16. `influence_depth_criterion`: `bearing_stress` (UFC 3-220-20 §6-5.8.4.3 p. 449, 10 % of the bearing stress of the equivalent footing, about 2.16 B' for a single pile; the default) or `effective_stress` (GEC-12 Vol I §7.3.5.3 step 1c p. 324). The pile's toe force, spread 2V:1H, counts to the chosen depth; a fill or drawdown below the toe counts to the bottom of the profile (neither rule cuts it off; UFC Eq 6-52). The result's `below_toe` gives each depth, why it stopped, both settlements and what the other rule gives (to_dict, summary, calc package, both adapters). The old fixed 3 B' is gone; a layer not marked settling below the toe is no longer compressed by the fill (as along the shaft). Also: a clay given Cc and e0 compresses under the toe load whether or not it is marked settling (it got 0 mm); a clay with C_ec and sigma_p but no C_er is refused by name; the GM-51 warning checks the toe zone only when the toe carries load. Numbers: DD-1, V-004 and UFC B-5.5 unchanged; a floating pile's head settlement 305.4 → 353.9 mm, a deep soft clay case 540.8 → 885.0 mm, toe in sand 13.88 → 12.75 mm (closed form 12.78); no neutral plane moved. Release note. Default rule: OWN-62. Commit `857b668`.
- **EV-21** (a deliverable cited a document no references tool returned): `funhouse_agent/cited_documents.py` finds document citations (GEC-n and its FHWA numbers as one document, AASHTO, UFC and NAVFAC DM 7 as the same documents, EM, ASTM, ACI, ASCE 7, Caltrans, NCHRP, CGPR, EC7, FEMA ... and author-year works) and checks them against what the conversation looked up, from the app's own record (the user's own words, references results and reference modules, worked examples, documents opened, never the files it wrote itself). Always recorded as a `cited_documents` event; behind `GEOTECH_CITED_DOCUMENTS` (OFF; `note` / `retry`, arms `cited_documents_note` / `cited_documents_retry`) a note on the answer or one retry, and a `cited_not_looked_up` line on deliverables (html_to_pdf and calc packages, write_docx, text save_file, annotate_document notes), the file always written. On wave w8b's record it flags GEC-11 and AASHTO LRFD 3.11.5 and not Rankine (1857); over 326 recorded smoke conversations, 12 flags, each a real unlooked-up citation. Parts landed in f5ffb16 alongside TA-33. Commit `ae53adb`.
- **TA-31** ("the library can" is not "this tool can"; a build not run says "not run"): `record_feedback`'s description says to re-check this app's tool (describe_method / list_methods) before recording a self-correction, and `save_file`'s that saving code does not run it, so a saved script is said not run, never checked (field session 2026-10-15; in the tools' own descriptions, not the system prompt).
- **GM-97** (the figures engineers make most; the owner's conventions, 2026-10-15: "use elevation as the default... most borings have a slightly different ground surface elevation"; "kPa and kN (everywhere!)"; past pressure "depends what level of analysis you're at"; "default to drawing the wall itself"): the figure checklist, `plot_code`'s and `plot_data`'s descriptions and the helpers' descriptions carry the four conventions (elevation rising upward by default, depth only when no elevations are given and said so; kPa and kN; test results as points, an assumed design profile as a line named as one; a wall or pile drawn to scale with its diagrams beside it, the diagrams alone only when the structure is not known); `spt_profile` replaces `depth_profile` (three borings at different ground levels, against elevation); three worked `plot_code` scripts (a wall with its pressure diagrams, a settlement stress profile, a pile design profile with the neutral plane) listed only when `plot_code` is offered, each drawn through the box with an empty layout check and looked at; labels in the references' terms (stress increase, consolidation tests, load and resistance curves).
- **TA-33** (a turn at the step cap ended on a raw error): the message is plain words (no LangGraph error name or documentation link); behind `GEOTECH_STEP_CAP_ANSWER` (OFF; arm `step_cap_answer`) the turn answers from what it gathered in one last call with no tools (`webapp.core.answer_at_step_cap`). Not measured live.
- **GM-104** (placeholder factors of safety reported as numbers): a rapid-drawdown search that finds no surface reports no FOS and says so, a search family's 999.9 marker never enters `candidates`, and `seismic_geotech` liquefaction refuses a zero peak acceleration as `liquepy_agent` already did (its FOS was 99.9 at every depth).
- **GM-105** (`soil_profile.to_retaining_wall_input` called `wall_height` the retained height): its docstring now names it as the wall module does, the total height from the underside of the base slab, used as the depth that splits backfill from foundation soil.
- **GM-103** (the cantilever calc package's sliding section printed the default basis): it prints the base friction angle, adhesion and passive resistance the analysis used (the module's own `check_sliding` gives them), saying when a value was given directly, so the printed R / P_h is the FOS shown; and a resultant beyond the middle third on the heel side now bears under the heel (it was drawn from the toe).
- **GM-100** (a cantilever wall reported a bearing check it never ran): with no `q_allowable` the bearing check is not run and says so everywhere (`FOS_bearing` and `passes_bearing` null, `checks_not_run`, "not checked" in the summary and the calc package); every cantilever and MSE result opens with a `verdict`, `checks_failed`, `checks_not_run` and `all_checks_pass`, failed checks first (the live w8 wall: "FAILS: sliding (FOS 1.17 < 1.50 required) ... Not checked: bearing"); `wall_height` is described as measured from the underside of the base slab to the top of the stem, and the result returns the stem height and base thickness. In the same check, and changing verdicts (release note): the bearing demand is the larger of the toe and heel pressures (it was the toe alone, unconservative for a resultant on the heel side), a resultant outside the base fails bearing (it passed at 99.9), and zero active thrust says "no driving force" instead of 99.9. MSE walls: the external bearing check had the same placeholder pass; in LRFD a Service I bearing ratio below 1 now fails when its resistance is given (V-009 still passes).
- **GM-62** (`analyze_staged` stopped plastic stages at 100 iterations): the default is 500 in the module and the `fem2d_staged` tool (a 260 kPa footing converges in about 500); a step that converges sooner stops sooner. Release note: staged runs that used to stop unconverged on a hard step now run on to convergence, taking longer on that step.
- **TA-30** (a result moved to scratch showed only its first 1,000 characters): a JSON result of 60,000 characters or more is written one key per line under a key map (every top-level key, the line it starts on, what it holds and its size) packed into the five lines deepagents' preview shows, with how to read one key (`read_file` offset, `grep`); the primary and every helper carry it, inside deepagents' own filesystem middleware (`funhouse_agent/deep/result_map.py`; field session 2026-10-15, kriging's 277,000-character result).
- **GM-16** (method descriptions over the 8,000-character cap): `describe_method` gives a method's documentation the reference budget (16,000), so the eight docs over 8,000 characters (six slope methods, up to 10,019, and downdrag's two) come back whole, prose and all; before, their prose was shortened to fit.
- **EV-05** (prompt caching on the Claude path): already in force: deepagents adds Anthropic's prompt-caching middleware to the primary agent and every helper (`module_work/live_smoke/models.py`), and waves w8 / w8b read 80-83 % of their prompt tokens from the cache; nothing to build.
- **GM-51** (no warning when nothing settles below the toe): downdrag warns when the soil in the toe's influence zone (3 pile diameters below the tip, the equivalent footing of UFC 3-220-20 Eqs 6-49 to 6-51) has no compressibility (clay without Cc/e0 or C_ec, sand without E_s) or is not in the profile at all, naming the spans and the settlement it counted (DD-1: the profile ends at the toe).
- **TA-32** (`write_xlsx` dropped a sheet with an unknown key without a word): a sheet entry's unknown keys are refused by name; `title` is read as the name and `columns` as the header row; a sheet with no rows is named in the warnings; the tool description says so (field session 2026-10-15).
- **VIS-28** (a look named a zoom tool the caller lacked): where `render_region` is not offered (the figure and calc helpers), a look's result carries no zoom hint naming it, and the tiling note drops the zoom sentence; a `zoom_note` says to ask again with finer tiles. Tiled looks now say to take small lettering from a tile and how things sit together (what overlaps or touches what) from the whole image (live wave w8).
- **GM-99** (`downdrag_package` took no `pile_area` or `pile_perimeter`): it now passes both through to the analysis, so a steel H or pipe pile's skin friction, A x E compression and own weight are its own, not a solid circle's; the package and the analysis tool agree for the same section (test).
- **GM-98** (plotting code in a locked box; approved by the owner on the figures page, 2026-10-15: "I've been frustrated by the calc packages' lack of creative figures, and now I'm guessing that the root cause is the lack of options the agent has. This would solve it... Please try to build it as securely as possible, don't do things that would trip the package up"; "Don't limit the run time too much, I want it to cook"): `plot_code` (`funhouse_agent/plot_box/`) runs matplotlib or Plotly code the model writes in a fresh Python process: a static check (imports only from numpy, pandas, matplotlib, mpl_toolkits, plotly, scipy and the standard maths and text modules; no `eval`, `exec`, `getattr` or dunder attributes), a scrubbed environment (no key or token reaches it), OS limits (Linux `resource`; a Windows job object), then an audit hook with no off switch over the code and the libraries it calls (reads in the conversation folder and the installed libraries; writes only figure, chart and data files in the folder; no network, processes or native code). The code gets `data`, `save_figure`, `save_chart` and the app's scale bar and north arrow; a figure left open is saved. 600 s unless the call asks for up to 3,600 s, 2,048 MB, two at once; while it runs the app's Working label says "drawing with code: 2m 10s so far". Behind `GEOTECH_PLOT_CODE` (OFF) for the figure and `calc` helpers; 38 tests including an escape battery run on Windows and Linux (f0bc000). Follow-up INF-16.
- **OWN-58** (answered on the desk 2026-10-15: "Leave it out by default, as the references do"): downdrag's load curve leaves the pile's own weight out by default, as UFC 3-220-20 §6-7.4 step 4 / Fig 6-26 and GEC-12 §7.3.6.1 step 4 do (both quoted in DESIGN.md); `include_pile_weight=True` keeps the old basis, and every result, summary and calc package names the basis. DD-1 headline 150.8 kN (was 174.9); V-004 54.00 ft / 486.6 / 285.6 kips (published 54 / 486 / 285). (867b08b). Follow-ups GM-99, DOC-18.
- **GM-50** (pile unit-weight defaults disagreed): `pile_unit_weight` defaults to steel (78.5) in the class, the tool and the calc package, as `pile_E` does; it matters only with the weight included (867b08b).
- **OWN-33** (answered on the desk 2026-10-15): the owner revoked the GitHub token saved in the old weekend QC routine and deleted the routine; the Monday drift canary stays (INF-12).
- **Backlog Desk: cards that take several answers** (owner on the desk, 2026-10-15: "you need to allow for multiple answers on some of these"): tick-boxes where options are not either/or, and one answer per part where a card asks two things (OWN-08, OWN-42) (e2e7c8e).
- **OWN-61** (answered 2026-10-15 on the figures page: "Go with B next, the figure helper. I assume the helper would be the Tufte checklist and some actual good looking example plots."): built as GM-92's first part, below; the owner's comments on the figures page the same evening are folded in (plans drawn as drawings, north from the source plan or up, z named, no coined labels).
- **GM-92, the figure helper built** (OWN-61 option B): a `figure` helper (`funhouse_agent/deep/figure_helper.py`) with an eleven-item figure checklist in its own prompt, in our own words, naming Tufte's principles (data-ink, chartjunk, small multiples, labels on the data) beside engineering conventions (plans to scale, depth down, units and coordinate system named, cases as markers, the quoted value marked); six worked examples on made-up data, each a complete `plot_data` call with why it is drawn that way (`profile_figure/figure_examples.py`: plan of borings, depth profile, sensitivity cases, settlement against time, load test, exceedance curve), fetched with `figure_examples`; `plot_data` to draw, `analyze_image` to look at the PNG (the same figure the chat shows), at most two redraws, `calculate` for any derived number; a 14-call budget. Behind `GEOTECH_FIGURE_HELPER` (arm `figure_helper`), OFF. `plot_data` itself improved while the examples were drawn: reference lines are named on the line (not in a legend), point names avoid lines and stay inside the frame, log axes read 1 / 10 / 100, and a legend below the plot sits under slanted case names instead of over the axis title. 8 new tests.
- **GM-92, plans drawn as drawings** (owner's comments on the figures page, 2026-10-15: "There isn't much use to the grid lines or the multiple axis number... a scale bar... a property line and north arrow any time we do a plan view"; "following Tufte principles: don't use unnecessary ink"; north "should follow what the plan you pulled data from uses or other plans you reviewed on a project. If there is not, it can default to north arrow up"; on the probability map: "need to define z"; on 'upper / persistent contact': "a classic case of LLM speak taking over for geotechnical-standard or geologic language"): `plot_data(plan_view=True, north_deg=, scale_units=)` draws no axes, numbers, grid or frame, a four-segment scale bar with the coordinate system under it and the north arrow beside it in a band below the site, a left-aligned title; north from the source plan, straight up with a note when not given; polygons take `hatch`; the shared `geotech_common/plan_furniture.py` draws the same furniture on the `gstools` probability map, which is now a plan too and names z (`value_name`, `value_unit`, `north_deg` on `conditional_simulation`); the examples use plain field terms and the checklist forbids coined labels. The live smoke harness now restores a flow's own switches (they leaked into the next flow). 13 new tests.
- **GM-94** (field session 2026-10-15: turn 4's footprint fractions were counted by eye from a 5 x 9 kriged grid, 10 of 40 wrong, and no tool turned a kriging variance into a probability): `calculate` takes list variables through count, sum, mean, median, std (sample), min, max, percentile(L, p) (numpy's linear rule), count_above(L, v) and count_below(L, v), refuses a list in arithmetic with a plain message and summarises a long list in its echo; the shared math list (`calculate` and `reliability`'s g-expressions) gains erf, erfc and the standard normal's norm_cdf, norm_pdf and norm_ppf. The description says to count, add up or average a list there, never by eye. 8 new tests.
- **GM-91** (field session 2026-10-15: the probability of exceedance of rock-excavation volume under a footprint could not be run: `gstools` had deterministic kriging, returning its whole grid as JSON, and an unconditioned random field only): `gstools.conditional_simulation` draws realizations that honour the observations and integrates each over plan polygons and levels on the same realizations (volume and area above or below a level, percentiles, P(V > 0), an exceedance table with standard errors, a convergence table, the kriged-surface and Gaussian pointwise totals beside them), with inequality data reported or rejection-sampled, the data's own statistics computed and echoed at run time, NaN realizations redrawn and counted, CSV files and two figures (exceedance curve; a plan map of P(z > level) drawn to scale with named borings), and no grids in the result; about a second for 1,000 realizations. **Method:** conditioning by kriging (Journel & Huijbregts 1978; Chilès & Delfiner 2012 §7.3.1) is the default. GSTools 1.7's `CondSRF` adds an unconditioned field scaled cell by cell by the kriging standard deviation, so each cell's spread is right but the correlation between cells is not the conditional one, and totals spread too much. For the SAME assumed model (simple kriging, exponential, 3 observations, 120 cells), keeping only the unconditioned fields that pass through the observations (conditioning by its definition, plain numpy, 4,000 kept of 159 million) gives a total standard deviation of 579, the Gaussian-conditioning formula 587, the default 581 and `CondSRF` 802 (lead's check after the owner asked how we know; now a test, `test_conditioning_by_definition.py`). "Right" is relative to the assumed model only; the model choices usually matter more; `simulation_method='condsrf'` stays to reproduce a CondSRF notebook. Kriging takes `regions` (area, expected volume and per-cell probability from the kriging mean and variance, said to be pointwise), writes a grid over 2,500 points to a CSV, and its description lists every key it returns. The descriptions tell kriging, random field and conditional simulation apart; `reliability.monte_carlo` points to it for a property mapped across a site. 64 new tests (139 in gstools_agent). Follow-ups GM-96.
- **GM-88** (fem2d SRM over a deep undrained foundation read about 4 % low on its default model, and the low-nu trap was only in a docstring): with no `x_extend` and undrained soil below the toe, the default level ground now reaches twice that soil's depth each side, with nx raised to keep the element size and a note saying so (Duncan Ex 1's model: 0.648 → 0.660; Taylor 0.679; run time about twice); an undrained layer with nu below 0.45 gets a warning asking for 0.49; `x_extend` also takes a (left, right) pair; the adapter names every SRM warning and the notes; V-054's docs now say the gap to the published 2.16–2.2 is mostly the model starting at the toe (2.237 with ground beyond). **Release note: default SRM runs over deep undrained soil give a slightly higher, more accurate FOS and take about twice as long.** 7 new tests. The criterion at the ceiling stays GM-93.
- **GM-95** (field session 2026-10-15: the helper that draws figures could not look at them, and a hand-written chart went out unseen while the agent checked a different PNG): the `calc` helper gets `analyze_image`; `save_file` adds a `chart_note` when it writes a `*.plotly.json` (no PNG of that figure exists; the PNG beside it, named, is now a different figure; use `plot_data`); `plot_data`'s description says its PNG and chart are one figure, so `analyze_image` on the PNG checks what the chat shows. 4 new tests.
- **TA-29** (field session 2026-10-15: the conversation could not be answered again after `StreamlitDuplicateElementKey`; chart text unreadable in the chat): a file rewritten in a later turn is carded in both turns, and the download button's key was `dlcard_<path>`, so the replayed transcript held two widgets with one key and Streamlit stopped the page. Each card's keys now carry its transcript entry and position (`dlcard_<entry>.<n>_<path>`, and the chart's `chart_<entry>.<n>_<path>`, since an identical chart shown twice fails the same way). Chart cards are drawn with `theme=None`, in the figure's own colours like its PNG: Streamlit's theme recoloured the text for the dark page, and on a chart's light paper the title, axis labels and legend vanished (the owner's screenshot). Tests: a two-turn rewrite and a rerun draw with unique keys (fails on the old code), and the chart's theme.
- **GM-90** (field session 2026-10-15: "annoying how the plots it's given aren't 1:1 in the axes"; the agent said `plot_data` could not fill a footprint, give marker shapes, name points or carry hover text, and a sensitivity study was drawn on a "case index" axis joined by lines): `plot_data` gains `equal_aspect` (one unit across as long as one up; the figure takes the data's proportions; legend outside the map), style `polygon` (closed, `fill`, `fill_opacity`, drawn under the points), per-series `marker` shapes, `marker_size`, `marker_edge`, `marker_open`, `line_dash`, `line_width`, `labels` (a name beside each point, placed clear of the other names and points, the same spot in the interactive twin), `hover` (per-point text in the interactive chart), `in_legend`, x given as names (cases along the x axis in order), `xlim` / `ylim` and `legend_position`. The interactive twin sets dark text on white paper itself. The tool's own description says to draw plans and sections to scale, name points on the figure and show separate cases as markers. 19 new tests, including the PNG's measured pixels per metre equal in x and y.
