- **GM-87** (the default slope search missed Taylor's deep base circle on long profiles: unsafe side): after the centre grid, its lowest minima (up to 3, within 10 %) are refined by a compass search on the centre with each centre's radius optimised as on the grid, inside the grid's own ranges (`refine=True` default on `search_critical_surface` and `search_rapid_drawdown`); rigorous methods walk with Bishop and the method re-scores the end (reinforced models walk with the method itself); circles may at most touch the base; the golden-section radius search reuses its kept point (26 % fewer solves). Taylor D = 2 with 4H / 5H: 0.739 / 0.755 → 0.703 (Taylor 0.706); D = 3: 0.690–0.703 → 0.676 (0.679). Every benchmark that moved went down (e.g. Duncan Ex 4 1.319 → 1.249 with the profile-end warning; #98 drawdown Corps 0.8370 → 0.8341); no published-benchmark test changed. The GM-63 profile-end check now also covers noncircular governing surfaces. Run time: Bishop +0.2–0.3 s, Spencer faster, drawdown +4–6 s.
- **GM-86** (the excavation result did not say where the wall's peak moment and shear are): each stage row gives `max_beam_moment_depth_m` / `max_beam_shear_depth_m`, and `wall_envelope` gives `moment_depth_m` / `shear_depth_m` with `moment_position` / `shear_position` (above / below / at the excavation level); the summary prints them, and the adapter's description says to quote them, not infer them. Live wave w6's case: the 44.6 kN·m/m peak at 3.83 m, above the excavation level (the agent had said about 8 m).
- **GM-63** (a deep undrained slope: fem2d SRM 0.648 against limit equilibrium 0.719 / 0.722): neither solver is wrong; both models were narrower than Taylor's problem, in opposite directions. Taylor's chart (FHWA NHI-06-088 Fig. 6-15, after Taylor 1948), checked by an independent φ = 0 circle optimisation: F = 0.679 for the 2H foundation (D = 3), its critical circle tangent to the firm base and leaving 2.69H beyond the toe. The LE test profile ended 1H past the crest and toe, so no trial circle could reach Taylor's: on 4H of level ground each side the search finds 0.676 (the LE solver scores Taylor's own circle exactly: unsafe side before). The SRM's side rollers sat 1.6H out, so the mechanism ran along them: with 1.5–2.5 times the clay depth each side it gives 0.660–0.668, 1.6–2.8 % below Taylor (expected for an upper-bound circle; safe side). New warnings, both in code: the slope search scores circles on the profile carried on level past its ends when the governing circle ends at a profile end, and warns with the lower FOS (fires on V-054: 2.237 against 2.286, toward the published 2.16–2.2); fem2d SRM warns when φ = 0 soil lies below the toe and the level ground is shorter than 1.5 times its depth. The firm-base case's "circles 0.969" in the docs corrected to 0.883. Follow-ups GM-87 (unsafe side), GM-88.
- **VIS-26, annotate and find_like on images** (a live run's phone-photo ring was drawn off the page twice: image pixels taken as page points): a new marked copy of an image is a PDF whose page is the image, upright, at 1 pt per pixel, so marks, the check's `found_bbox` and later looks all speak the image's pixels (an existing copy at another scale is converted exactly); a mark centred off the image (or off a PDF page) refuses the call, naming the size; `find_like` searches an image at its own resolution with EXIF applied and returns pixel boxes; `open_document` on an image says its coordinates are pixels; the per-turn note no longer lists an image zoom as "p. 1". A rebuild of the live photo: before, the ring at [1040, 44, 1065, 443] on the page edge (as live); after, round the note, confirmed first time. In the tools' own descriptions only. 14 new tests.
- **GM-84** (OpenSees site response accepted unconverged steps on small elements): the dynamic stage now runs Newton at NormDispIncr 1e-3 m (the authors' total-stress example's algorithm and test), falling back to KrylovNewton then ModifiedNewton at a test 100 times tighter, then 10 substeps, then an error naming the time; Newton needed no fallback in 25 runs. Wiki column: amplification against the port from −35…−9 % to within 0.1 %, PGA from +5 % to −0.1 %; the strict expected-failure marker is gone. Run time +15 % on the 160-element column. Kramer, rock-impedance and pystrata benchmarks unchanged.
- **GM-85** (the elastoplastic gravity stage failed on finer sand meshes): elements are built at 1 m/s permeability for gravity, then each element's own value (sand 1e-4, clay 1e-7 m/s) is set through `parameter` / `updateParameter` before the clock reset, as the OpenSeesPy effective-stress example does (checked: an update to the same value changes nothing; without it the column shakes drained). The effective column now runs (Sa −6.2 to +4.5 % against the port; r_u 0.36 / 0.89 / 0.98 against 0.38 / 0.91 / 0.97) and the rebuilt failing cases finish; the marker is gone. 134 opensees tests pass in WSL.
- **GM-83** (pin OpenSees site response to the OpenSees wiki's own example): McGann & Arduino's "Site Response Analysis of a Layered Soil Column (Total Stress Analysis)" (University of Washington, OpenSees wiki) and the OpenSeesPy docs' effective-stress example (14.4.2) ported to openseespy as test helpers; the ports reproduce the published figures (surface PGA 0.353 g at 4.43 s against about 0.35 g near 4.5 s; Sa(0.4 s) 1.38 g against about 1.4 g; peak r_u 0.43 / 0.97 / 0.56 against about 0.42 / 0.97 / 0.55). Linear: the function within 3 % in amplification and 2.7 % in PGA. Nonlinear: pinned as strict expected failures for the two defects found, GM-84 and GM-85. The Tcl does reset the clock (`setTime 0.0`). Input: the first 20 s of the example's Gilroy No. 1 EW record (25.8 KB, derived, header says how). In WSL 125 passed, 2 expected failures. Gaps in what a user can set: GM-79.
- **GM-81, single surfaces** (a single-surface Spencer / GLE with no solution showed the unreinforced value as the method's FOS): `SlopeStabilityResult` gains `warnings` (reverse curvature with its crack depth, short arm with Spencer's value on that circle, no solution, a noncircular Bishop switched to Spencer), `method_solved`, `method_used`, `methods_without_solution`; when supports cross a surface and the method finds no solution, `FOS` is None (the legacy solver ignores supports, so its number belongs to a different slope: the anchored #85 toe circle showed 0.891, the slope without its 9,000 lb/ft support) and the calc package prints "No stability check"; without supports the legacy value stays, labelled as such; a search whose governing circle came from the legacy solver says so. Searches unchanged. Slope suite (597), calc package and 378 consumer tests pass.
- **EV-18, built** (an answer and a DIGGS file cited pages nobody had read): `funhouse_agent/cited_pages.py` builds the conversation's record of pages read or looked at from the app's own record (the activity log of the primary and its helpers across turns, the coverage ledger, `reads.json`, `page_readings.json`) and parses citations (PDF pages, bare and printed page labels, "Pages" table columns; not fooled by "a 40-page report" or "Table 5-2"; printed labels matched through planlens' page map; another publication's pages and structure-tool page runs left out). `write_diggs` always adds a `page_not_read` cross-check entry per record citing pages no tool opened (never refusing) and a `cited_pages` result; `record_finding` returns `pages_not_opened`; after each turn a `cited_pages` event is recorded, and with `GEOTECH_CITED_PAGES` (OFF) the answer gets a note or the model one retry. On the field session's record it flags exactly the four 1987 placeholder records and none of the twelve real ones (checked locally). 55 new tests.
- **GM-73, built** (an excavation below the water table was modelled kept full of water, so the wall carried no net water pressure: unsafe for a dewatered dig): `analyze_excavation(excavation_water='dewatered')` is now the DEFAULT (UFC 3-220-20 (2025) p. 97, groundwater control below the table; Caltrans T&S Manual (2025) Ex 7-2; FHWA GEC 4 (1999) §5.2.9 Eqs 15-17; EM 1110-2-2504 ¶4-5): the water inside lowered to the excavation level each lift, the table held outside, steady seepage under the impermeable wall's tip (`porewater.seepage_heads`, T3 / T6 / Q4); `'full'` (the old model) and `'level'` (an inside level given) stay as options; per-layer `k` (ratios matter); the result gives the tip pore pressure and head, the net water pressure at the excavation level beside the no-seepage and GEC 4 values, the upward gradient in front of the wall with a quick-condition warning, and `wall_water_pressures`. Pinned: the seepage solver against the exact conformal-mapping solution for a sheet pile in a layer (within 1 % of the head difference), and GEC 4 §5.2.9's propped sheet pile (net pressure between Eq 16 and Eq 17, tip pressure 6 % above Eq 15; a flow net eight times larger agrees within 2 %). Default clay case, table at the ground, strut at 1.5 m: M_max 16.2 → 45.9 kN·m/m, strut 23.7 → 80.8 kN/m. **Release note: any call with a table above the excavation level now gives larger wall forces (the default changed).** The rest stays GM-73.
- **TA-28, built** (an interrupted turn's files and earlier page readings did not reach the helpers): `recover_partial` now attaches the files an interrupted turn changed in `files/` since it started (`checkpoint_partial` keeps the start time) as the recovered entry's artifacts, so they get download cards, and `working_files_note` names them as that turn's ("interrupted before it answered"; "stopped on an error") and lists up to 10 other unnamed folder files. A new read-only tool `kept_readings` lists the conversation's kept page readings by reference (page, kind of look, what it was asked, length, a one-line summary; 25 a call) and returns one whole by document and page, as `reuse=true` does; it is on the geotech page for the primary and its helpers, and on the review page only behind `GEOTECH_REVIEW_KEPT_READINGS` (arm `kept_readings`, OFF until the suite measures it). When to use it lives only in its own description. 15 new tests, including a compiled agent whose helper lists and takes an earlier reading.
- **INF-07** (master CI not green for months): the GitHub Tests run on 76e8a56 (2026-10-15, run 38657616268) finished green on Python 3.12 and 3.13 in about 50 minutes, the first full pass in months. What it took: the 5 tests that failed every run (9152c66), the workflow rework, and GM-77 (an OpenSees test that hung every run until the time limit). The next run (1d0a8f6) failed only on two backlog-ID checks from a commit pushed before its check ran, fixed in 741e680 (15,392 passed). A failure email now means a real failure.
- **GM-59** (slope supports in Bishop; with GM-60, GLE / Spencer with large point loads): GM-59 was partly misdiagnosed: on Slide2 #85 the 1.304 Bishop search came from a reverse-curvature toe circle closed by a dry tension crack (Slide2's own default construction, which its grid never generates); with such circles discarded (new `SlopeGeometry.reverse_curvature='discard'`; default `'tension_crack'`, unchanged, with a search warning) the module gives 1.503 active / 1.305 passive against Duncan & Wright 1.51 / 1.32 and Slide2 1.531 / 1.324. Fixed against Wright & Duncan (1991, TRR 1330): Bishop now puts a support's vertical component in its slice's vertical equilibrium and its moment with sign; Fellenius adds the normal component to N; `support_convention` ('active' = Method A, default; 'passive' = Method B, T/F, per the Slide2 help) on nails, anchors, geosynthetics and piles in every method; a warning when a Bishop / OMS critical circle's supports act with a short arm (on #85: 29 % counted). GM-60: Spencer and GLE now solve each fixed-λ state with supports by a slice-by-slice march (Zhu et al. 2005) and a bracketed root, the λ search steps over missing states and tries its bounds: on Slide2's #85 circle Bishop = Spencer = GLE 1.564 (were 0.913, the unreinforced fallback); unreinforced runs unchanged. Follow-ups GM-81, GM-82, OWN-59, OWN-60.
- **GM-60** (GLE / Spencer failed to converge with large point loads): built with GM-59, see the entry above.
- **RI-31, built** (the primary retyped 29k characters of helper output into `write_diggs`, and four kinds of fault came in that way): `write_diggs` takes `investigations_file` / `lab_tests_file` (one JSON file or a list, from the conversation's working folder only; a list of objects, one object or `{investigations: [...]}`), checked as inline records are with each problem naming its file; inline and file inputs combine, an `investigation_id` in two inputs is refused naming both, and the result lists each input's `sources`. Every `task` result whose helper saved files gets a code-written note naming them ("never retype what it holds"); the helper's partial-answer rule allows a saved file's name in place of the data. An end-to-end offline test: a helper saves its records, the primary passes the file, the DIGGS file is valid and reads back equal. The rest stays RI-31.
- **PL-24, samples** (`log_grid` gave no sample intervals, and 7 of 8 results stopped a few metres down a log): planlens 14dac18 reads each sample's box off the lines drawn across the sample columns through the log's own ruler (top, bottom, plus_minus, how read, what the columns print inside), ignoring contacts, water lines, dashed grid and scale lines and joining boxes split by a sheet break; rows come compact by default so a whole log fits one result (a synthetic 30 m, 3-sheet log: 21 of 21 intervals within ±0.011 m in 8.5k characters; the field session's 8 logs whole in one call each, 58 of 59 hand-truthed SPT intervals within 0.015 m, offline). Reaches the app with a planlens release (PL-22); the app side is feature-detected. The rest stays PL-24.
- **RI-28, the wording and cross-checks**: `write_diggs`'s description says a depth read off the log's own drawn scale is read from the log and is written with its reading noted, and that a record with its values in hand is never held back (only what could not be read is left out, and named); keys for increment, recovery, RQD; the system prompt's "values as printed" became "values read off the logs". `reconcile` cross-checks drives against the sample interval, the drive count against the hole's form, and any reading below the total depth (never refusing): the field session's turn-3 calls now give 3 entries (were 0), its turn-2 file flags a layer at 21.1 m below a 20.1 m hole. The rest stays RI-28.
- **TA-27, built** (the launcher with a Prompter key, and an app the notebook lost): `run_on_databricks(prompter_key=dict(url=..., model=..., api_key=...))` sets the key's settings in the app's environment only (never printed, never in the bootstrap script), boots `tinyapps_entry.py`, offers that one model and names it in the banner (the old four-line cell still works and now names the right model); `running_apps()`, `stop_running(pid)` (SIGTERM, then SIGKILL) and `diagnose(pid)` (open connections, what each thread waits on, the log tail with key-like text hidden) find and handle a copy the notebook lost, using only /proc and `ss`; an explicit port held by a lost copy raises `PortInUse` with the ways out, the default port moves on and names the old copy. 31 offline tests (fake /proc, fake processes); live check stays TA-27.
- **INF-14** (class-scoped fixtures written as instance methods, which pytest 10 removes): 14 fixtures in 9 test files (concrete_props_agent, fem2d, pynite_agent, section_props_agent, slope_stability, soe) are now `@classmethod`s, as pytest's deprecation note asks; the fem2d cross-validation fixtures read their class constants through `cls`. 180 tests pass.
- **GM-76, axis labels** (the plan view always said Easting / Northing): the DIGGS reader now keeps each exploration's coordinate system (the `srsName` our writer puts on `gml:pos`, or on the point), and the site's when all agree; `plot_plan_view` labels longitude / latitude for a geographic system, easting / northing for a projected one (UTM, State Plane, any other EPSG code), and plain x / y with the system named under the title for a local grid or none stated. The rest stays GM-76.
- **GM-78** (`pystrata_agent` always reported not converged): pystrata's relative error is in percent, so the fraction 0.01 asked for a 0.01 % change and every equivalent-linear run used all its iterations (the same comparison is in pystrata's own loop). The wrapper now passes the tolerance in percent and judges convergence on the size of the change (pystrata's `max_error` keeps its sign, so a stiffening layer could read as converged). The test profile now converges in 6 iterations at 1 %; `tolerance` is documented as a fraction.
- **GM-77** (OpenSees site response gave almost no surface motion; sand over clay hung CI): `opensees_agent.analyze_site_response` had seven defects, all fixed: the clock was left at 10,000 s after gravity, past the input record (now `ops.setTime(0.0)`, as the OpenSees effective-stress site response example does); gravity loaded the column with 10,000 times its weight (nodal loads on a Linear series with 500 s steps; now the element body force, effective stress within 0.05 % of hand values), which is what hung sand over clay; the PressureDependMultiYield02 arguments were one slot off (now in the documented order, the wiki table's Dr = 40 % column: phase-transformation angle 26°, e = 0.77); the base force was 2 x c x v_outcrop (Joyner & Chen 1975: c x v_outcrop for an outcrop motion); su went in as the clay's cohesion (simple-shear peak 2c/√3; now c = √3/2 su); the node at the water table was left free; dry elements reported ru. The analysis runs in a fresh child Python with `time_limit_s` (default 600 s), since a stuck OpenSees step holds the GIL. Pinned: Kramer (1996) §7.2 uniform damped layer on elastic rock (amplification 4.178 vs 4.169 at the site frequency; 3 % tolerance), a rock-impedance column reproduces the outcrop motion (1.0006), pystrata linear elastic PGA 0.749 vs 0.758 g, and nonlinear vs pystrata equivalent-linear on the same backbone (within 20 % at 0.05 g; below it at 0.30 g). Uniform sand 0.30 g: surface PGA 8.5e-7 g → 0.150 g (liquefies). 116 opensees tests pass in WSL. Follow-ups GM-78–GM-80.
- **INF-10** (module suites on a fresh resolve): Python 3.12 in WSL, core + `[pydiggs]` (`[ags4]` still caps pandas below 3), numpy 2.5.4, pandas 3.0.6, scipy 1.18.1, matplotlib 3.11.2, plotly 7.1.0, streamlit 1.66.0, langchain 1.3.18, deepagents 0.7.13, openai 2.54.0: about 15,360 passed, 39 skipped, over every package folder one at a time. One failure, fixed: deepagents 0.7.x adds a `delete` scratch tool, which reached the review page's menu, its reading helper and the references consult; it is hidden with the others. Found: GM-77 (OpenSees site response, which also hangs CI), INF-13, INF-14. The streamlit tested range is now `<1.67`.
- **GM-71** (`fem2d.analyze_excavation` did not model an excavation: the wall moved into the retained soil): rebuilt on the staged path. Half of a symmetric excavation (`width` is now the full width, the right boundary its centre line); stage 0 sets in-situ stresses by the K0 procedure (the layer's `K0`, else Jaky 1 − sin φ′) with no movement; then one stage per lift removes the lift's elements and releases the forces they carried; the wall is wished in place and each strut installed stress-free when the excavation reaches it (`lifts`, default one to each strut level, then to depth). `wall_contact='smooth'` (default, δ = 0) cuts the soil along the wall. New results: `excavation_stages`, `wall_envelope` (largest M and V over all stages, the design values), `notes`; displacements count from the end of stage 0. Benchmark: the USS Steel Sheet Piling Design Manual's cantilever in granular soil (pp. 86–90; the hand method reproduces the printed D0 and M_max within 1.3 %), applied with δ = 0: FEM M_max within 3 % on the default mesh (±10 % test tolerance); the retained soil reaches Ka. Default clay case: head u_x −6.1 → +6.7 mm, M_max 17.2 → 27.6 kN·m/m; with a strut, its force −37.6 → +52.7 kN/m. `analyze_staged` gains `springs`, `initial_stresses`, `settled_ratio`, and `water_on_free_faces=True` (water standing on a free face below the table; before, the pore pressure pushed such a face outward unbalanced). Found on the way: eval key MRS-1 still held the SRM value from before GM-05 (1.531; limit equilibrium on that slope gives 1.17, Bishop 1.173, Spencer 1.171); re-pinned to 1.169 / 1.181. Follow-ups GM-72–GM-75, GM-62.
- **GM-61** (a beam or spring placed in a later stage carried the movement its nodes made before it existed): members are placed stress-free and carry only later movement (`lin_offset` in the solver); a rotation DOF no active beam touches is fixed. Tested by a wall installed after gravity with nothing else changed (with GM-71).
- **TA-26** (a model request had no time limit): the continue turn hung 3.5+ hours on ONE reviewer model request the gateway never answered (activity log: `model_start` at 9.6 min, nothing after). langchain-openai and langchain-anthropic give their SDK client `timeout=None` (no limit) unless told, and the OpenAI client passes its own timeout on every request, so the Prompter route's httpx 180 s never applied. Now the Prompter route's ChatOpenAI carries `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S` (180 s; that session's 989 answered calls took 106 s at most) and the Foundry and Anthropic routes `MODEL_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S` (600 s, the SDKs' own default); a timeout is then retried as busy (TA-20). Tested against a server that accepts and never answers. Confirmed on the cluster: the stuck thread sat in `read()` on the open connection to the Prompter host, every other thread idle. Vision side calls already had their own clock.
- **TA-25** (the `reviewer` helper had no model-call budget, and was handed data QA it cannot do): in the field session's continue turn (5.33.0) the primary asked the reviewer to QA the DIGGS file against the report. The reviewer now gets a 10-call budget like a references consult (`DEFAULT_REVIEWER_MAX_MODEL_CALLS`), its last call answering from what it has; its description says "only after a draft answer that involved calculations", and that it sees only its brief and cannot open documents, pages or files. Data QA against a source stays GM-43. The hang itself was TA-26.
- **GM-70** (fem2d excavation walls built as a zigzag with T6 elements): `create_wall_elements` takes only the node column nearest the wall line, from the node nearest the top to the node nearest the tip; `analyze_excavation` snaps the nearest column onto x = 0 and the nearest row onto the wall tip before the midside nodes are made. Default 30x15 T6: M_max 91.6 → 17.2 kN·m/m, V_max 158.0 → 28.0; a strut −67.0 → −36.6 kN/m; T3 and T6 now agree within 1.4 % in head deflection and 0.8 % in moment above the excavation on 60x30. No test asserted a wall number. Found underneath: GM-71.
- **GM-39** (Ensoft-style calculation tables, the owner's next module train): `CalcTable` / `get_tables()`, `calc_package.export_tables`, one CSV per table (units in the headers) plus a printed "Calculation Tables" section; lateral pile, axial pile, pile group, drilled shaft, settlement, bearing capacity, sheet pile (cantilever and anchored), slope stability (result and search), downdrag, fem2d (FEM, staged, consolidation), liquefaction, the wave-equation bearing graph, ground-improvement time curves. Bugs found and fixed on the way: GM-68, GM-69, GM-70, upside-down sheet-pile figures. Left: GM-41 (SOE package), GM-66, GM-67; retaining walls and pavement hold only scalars.
- **VIS-09** (no zoom for pasted screenshots and image uploads): `render_region` accepts an image (`bbox`, `view` + `image_box`, `turn`, `reuse`, `marks` in the image's own pixels, EXIF rotation applied) and `analyze_image` gains `tiles` and `turn`; every crop is cut from the original and enlarged only by whole numbers, sized to the model's budget; described only in the tools' own text. `analyze_pdf_page` on an image file now looks at it as an image (it used to give a view in points at 0.75 pt per pixel). A 3840 x 2160 screenshot's fine print: unreadable → read on tile r1c2, box 2.2 px off; a zoom on it reads it, 0.3 px off. Live on Claude Sonnet (2026-10-14, LTQ check 9, $0.11): the tag sheet as a 3978 x 2574 PNG, look and tiles read 27/28 tags (6 boxed loosely round the whole callout), zooms 8/8 within 0.7 px; the rollout model is brief 6 part A.
- **RI-27** (DIGGS: not-encountered water written as a level, unknown positions as 0, a printed ">50" refusing the file): one rule shared by writer and gate (`_water_readings`): `not_encountered` is never a reading (a borehole gets `notEncountered`, a pit a fixed remark; a depth given with it is listed in `written.normalised`); the reader reports `gwl_not_encountered`. An unknown x, y or elevation is written as NaN (DIGGS requires `referencePoint`; NaN is schema-valid and cannot be mistaken for a place), listed in `written.assumed` / `position_unknown`, read back as None (site model fields Optional; plots list what they skipped). A printed refusal notation in `n` (`>50`, `50/5"`, `R`, ...) becomes `refusal: true` with `n_printed` kept and written; genuinely invalid values are still refused. The gate now catches all three. Follow-ups RI-30.
- **EV-14** (the record could not answer cost, cause or who did what): token use on the Prompter route (langchain-openai turns `stream_usage` off for a custom base_url; now on, with a fallback if the gateway refuses `stream_options`); `task_id` and the helper's brief on every event inside a helper (vision calls from its tools too); `model_error` carries status, retry-after, x-ratelimit headers and the ladder's tries, and an httpx hook lists every HTTP try inside a call; engine and retry policy in `turn_start` and `meta.json` (never a key or an address, tested); queue time for vision slots and the document lock; cut payloads kept whole in `activity_full/`; vision events labelled with kind, page, tile, image size and their enclosing tool. Leftovers: EV-16.
- **TA-20** (agent model calls had no busy ladder): `AgentBusyRetry` (outermost model-call middleware on the primary and every sub-agent, all builds) retries a busy refusal (429, 529, 5xx, overloaded, connection, timeout) on the provider's retry-after (1-120 s) or 15/30/60/120 s with jitter, 5 tries (`GEOTECH_AGENT_BUSY_TRIES`), never a spent budget, within a per-turn bound (`GEOTECH_BUSY_TURN_MAX_S`, 900 s); each wait is logged as `model_busy` with status, retry-after and rate-limit headers; the error after the ladder says how many tries and how long. Tested on the Prompter key route (real ChatOpenAI over a mock transport) and the Funhouse bridge.
- **TA-22** (parallel helpers: no cap, no pacing, no budget): at most `GEOTECH_MAX_HELPERS` (2) helpers at once; a per-key token pacer over agent and vision calls (`GEOTECH_TOKENS_PER_MINUTE`, off until the deployment's limit is known; keys kept only as a fingerprint); general-purpose helpers get a 30-call budget whose last call hands over the data gathered. Leftovers: TA-24.
- **TA-19** (an errored turn kept none of its work): every AI reply and tool result is recorded as it arrives (`webapp/turn_record.py`, checkpointed to `partial_messages.json`); on an error the record becomes replayable history in valid order (a call that never returned gets a "Not finished" result) with a closing note to the model to pick up from it, and a process killed mid-turn has the checkpoint folded in on the next open. Results over 60k characters (or the largest while the kept set exceeds 300k) are kept whole in the conversation's `kept/` folder with their first 4,000 characters in the history. The error text says exactly what is kept ("Nothing is lost" is gone). Live check: GPT replayed on its own earlier tool calls.
- **TA-21** (a stopped helper returned nothing): every helper's model replies and tool results are recorded; a helper that raised, hit its budget or gave up after reading ≥ 20k characters writes `kept/helper_<brief>_<id>.md` (brief, why kept, a code-made summary, an index of every result, every step in full) and the primary gets the path and the summary. The general-purpose prompt asks helpers to return the data gathered and what is missing, never only an apology.
- **GM-69** (anchored sheet pile: maximum moment overstated by T·a): the shear starts at zero and T enters at the anchor depth; pressures below the excavation are integrated and the maximum is searched over the whole wall; D and T unchanged (bit-identical). Pinned to the USS Steel Sheet Piling Design Manual, Design Example No. 2, pp. 101-102 (D 6.92 ft vs 6.96, T 3,188 lb/ft vs 3,193; M_max 15,649 ft-lb/ft vs 15,773 by hand from the printed pressure diagram, -0.8 %; old code +100 %). Old → new M_max, kN·m/m: φ30 H6 case 252.3 → 138.9; V-013 classical 596.5 → 187.1. Also: every sheet-pile calc-package diagram was drawn upside down (axis inverted twice) and recomputed its own shear/moment; both fixed, figures now draw the result's own profile.
- **VIS-01** (turned lettering read upright): the text-layer turn (c9febc8), an optional `turn` (0/90/180/270) on `analyze_pdf_page` and `render_region` described only in the tools' own descriptions (ON), and the automatic turned re-read with per-page memory behind `GEOTECH_VISION_TURN_RETRY` (OFF, arm `turn_retry`). Every measured failure was stroke-lettered; the suite's set-long-rare-tag and Mecklenburg tasks measure it (EV-13; the list of what to count is in `module_work/harness_theory/locating_things_on_a_page.md`).
- **GM-68** (found and fixed 2026-10-14 in the tables train: `pile_group_package` dropped the axial / lateral stiffness and batter, so `method_6dof` always raised): it now builds piles and loads through the pile-group adapter; a missing `axial_stiffness` is named.
- **VIS-05** (boxes on sideways sheets): every location request carries one general sentence (give boxes in the image as sent, even where the drawing is turned) and every conversion takes the page's /Rotate and any view turn. /Rotate 0-270 x lettering 0-270: worst box-centre error 0.38 pt; the brief-5 failure shape 130 pt off → 0.38 pt.
- **VIS-04** (a zoom that cuts text lines invents the rest): every look ends with one sentence (quote only what is visible, write (cut), never complete); `render_region` widens the window to take in the lines it cuts (≤ a quarter a side, never past 300 pt for a mark-anchoring window; `window_widened`), and lines still cut are named to the call with the edge that cuts them (`edge_note`). All measured cases were stroke-lettered, so only the sentence reaches them; EV-13 measures it.
- **PL-07** (distinct stroke-drawn sheets flagged as duplicates, 22 of 24): planlens 89711ea. Words name a repeat only with 15+ words outside the header/footer bands; every nominated repeat must also render the same (hashes within 2 bits, a ~36 dpi render within 16 grey levels everywhere); `duplicate_of` names the earliest page. 22 → 0 on the suite set; true repeats still caught; page-map time unchanged. Reaches the app with PL-22.
- **DR-17** (the duplicate-mark note): says a repeat usually means one of the things meant is still unmarked (planlens 89711ea; PL-22).
- **DR-04** (geotech tools on the default review page): `read_reference_figure` and `view_worked_example_source` are filtered out when `review_page` is set (tools 35 → 33); a test pins the page's tool list. The generic deepagents prompt is DR-29.
- **DR-15** (GPT-5.4 stopped after two rejected markups): checks 1-3 of a marked copy say to keep correcting without asking, check 4 says to hand the file over naming every unconfirmed mark (`MAX_CORRECTION_ROUNDS` = 4); the `annotate_document` description says the same; no system-prompt change. Unmeasured live: EV-13.
- **DR-16** (return the corrected box): a misplaced ring or rectangle whose look found the named thing carries `found_bbox` in page points, and the note says to re-mark with it; a ring 30 pt off is re-marked and confirmed in one step.
- **GM-20** (SOE free earth support returned a depth with no solution): when no moment balance exists within 6H, the embedment and everything computed from it come back null with `converged=False` and a plain note in the Caltrans T&S terms; every caller handles it (`compute_embedment`, both excavation analyses, the FHWA single-anchor branch keeps the load above the anchor). One existing test asserted the bug (`test_braced_soft_clay`: 4c < γH + q) and now expects no balance.
- **GM-42** (no catalog headroom): 38 module briefs rewritten, keeping method and standard names; `list_agents` 7,988 → 7,217 characters (cap 8,000).
- **GM-30** (Terzaghi bearing capacity): `factor_method="terzaghi"` (and `bearing_capacity_factors(method="terzaghi")`, the calc package), general or local shear, UFC 3-220-20 Table 5-5 shape factors, no depth or inclination factors (inclined loads and slopes refused, UFC Table 5-3). N-gamma: Kumbhojkar (1993) as tabulated in Das by default, Coduto et al. (2016, the basis of UFC Table 5-2) as the alternative, both named in every result. Matches UFC Table 5-2 at every row except four misprints (REF-21) and Das's table within 0.5 %. Also fixed: the `bearing_capacity/terzaghi` alias silently returned a Vesic answer.
- **RI-26** (found and fixed 2026-10-14, from a Funhouse run): an agent passed a printed SPT N of ">50" to `write_diggs` as `n`, was refused on 17 rows and concluded refusal could not be written. The writer already records refusal (`refusal: true`, the drives in `blows` as printed, e.g. `'50/5"'`); the refusal message for a text `n` now says so. Tests pin the hint and a refusal written that way (valid DIGGS, read back equal).
- **GM-05** (SRM factor of safety depended on the mesh): two causes. (1) `generate_slope_mesh` kept the whole Delaunay hull, so the notch above the toe was meshed as sliver "soil" that propped the face (25 % extra area on GL99 Ex 1); triangles outside the section are now dropped. (2) A residual that stalled while the displacements stood still was read as collapse; failure now needs non-convergence with displacements still growing (Griffiths & Lane's definition). Every benchmark now varies ≤ 1.7 % across meshes once ≥ 4 elements span the slope and lies within +2.5 / −5.4 % of limit equilibrium: the 10 m 2:1 slope 3.000 / 1.803 / 1.147 on three meshes → 1.909 / 1.897 / 1.884 (LE 1.887); GL99 Ex 1 1.331 on every mesh (published 1.4, LE 1.368); ACADS 1(a) 0.949 (published 1.00). Results warn plainly on too few elements across the slope, an unmeshed or too-thin layer, a dry cohesionless face above tan φ′/tan β, a range that did not bracket failure; ν ≥ 0.5 is refused; `mesh_check=True` re-runs 1.5x finer and reports both.
- **GM-06** (undrained locking): measured, there is none: GL99 Ex 4 gives 1.419 at ν 0.30 through 0.4999 (Taylor 1.47, LE 1.486). The old +19-26 % was the soil-filled notch. B-bar was built, measured worse (Ex 4 1.33, Prandtl Nc 4.52 against 5.14) and removed; no ν warning, since ν does not move the answer.
- **GM-08** (`analyze_staged` ignored `n_steps`): a stage now ramps from its start state to its full load over `n_steps` (a 360 kPa footing: one step finds no equilibrium, 10 steps settle 68 mm, matching ten 36 kPa stages to 0.1 %). Elastic stages unchanged. Also fixed in passing: the Duncan Ex 1 test compared a deep-foundation model with a shallow-base publication (rewritten: SRM 0.883 = non-circular Spencer 0.883); a calc-steps table title no longer says "Judgment". fem2d suite 461 passed.
- **GM-53** (the critical-surface search ignored reinforcement): every trial surface in every search kind (grid, entry/exit, random and DE noncircular, PSO, weak layer, the toe and infinite-slope families, fixed-surface FOSM / Monte Carlo, all three rapid-drawdown stages) now carries nails, anchors, geosynthetics and stabilizing piles exactly as `analyze_slope` does; the result lists the reinforcement crossing the critical surface. A search rejects a reinforced surface on which Spencer / GLE does not converge (the old fallback dropped the reinforcement and could win). Toe circles may touch a rigid base but not dip under it. ACADS 1(a) + 200 kN/m geosynthetic 0.990 → 1.123; Slide2 #86 with tiebacks now reproduces (Bishop 1.612, Spencer 1.606, GLE 1.606 against Slide2 1.629 / 1.620 / 1.622 and Duncan & Wright 1.61); #85 GLE 1.581 against 1.575 (its Bishop is GM-59). V-043's 44-56 kN/m is a single-surface quantity and its test stands. Runtime on reinforced slopes +11-41 %; unreinforced unchanged.
- **GM-54** (drawdown searches missed toe circles): `search_rapid_drawdown` runs the toe-circle and infinite-slope families with the drawdown FOS and respects given ranges; a free-draining c′ = 0 shell gives the planar closed form (0.942). At 2H:1V Pilarcitos now finds the shallow face slide classic under rapid drawdown (DWW 0.860, LK 1.017); the published values describe the observed deep slide, so `test_v052_pilarcitos_method_ordering_is_slope_invariant` runs with `infinite_slope=False` (explained in the test and INVENTORY). Automatic-range drawdown searches +40-60 % runtime.
- **GM-55** (the slope calc package dropped inputs silently): `_build_slope_geom` builds layers through the slope adapter's `build_soil_layer` (unknown keys refused; ru, strength_model and the SHANSEP / Hoek-Brown / anisotropic / R-envelope fields pass through), the report checks its variables, and the response and report show the governing surface, each family's minimum, the reinforcement crossing it, the infinite-slope checks and the search warnings; `geo_project` stores a planar critical surface by its points instead of xc = yc = R = 0. The fixed-circle `slope_stability_package` is strict about layer keys too now.
- **GM-14** (c′ = 0 slopes: the circle search missed the planar failure): wherever c′ = 0 soil forms a face the search now also scores shallow planar surfaces parallel to it (Janbu simplified, 0.1-0.4 % above the infinite-slope closed form with ru and kh). #86 without supports: 1.566 → 0.944 (true 0.942). Duncan Ex 2/3/4 now governed by the infinite slope (1.682, 1.197, 2.347 against closed forms 1.678, 1.194, 2.344). Results name the governing surface (`governing_surface`, `candidates`, `infinite_slope_checks`) and warn plainly. `slope_stability/search_supplements.py`.
- **GM-13** (small toe circles under-sampled): circles through each slope face's toe are scored after the main search (Bishop scan, the best three re-optimised in the chosen method). Steep φ = 0 case 2.784 → 2.286 (true 2.287); coarse-grid tests now match fine grids (Duncan Ex 6 1.716, V-029 1.130). Every number that moved went down. V-032 (Slide2 #61) re-pinned to 1.315 with brackets: Slide2's own printed circle gives 1.324 here and 1.3244 by an independent hand Bishop, so the published 1.366 is not reproducible from the stated inputs (recorded in INVENTORY/RESULTS). Runtime +0-12 %.
- **GM-52** (found and fixed 2026-10-14: the slope adapter silently dropped unknown keys inside soil layers, so `"c": 10` became c′ = 0; seen live in smoke wave 1, where an agent's Bishop check reported 0.97 against the true 1.89): `check_nested_keys` now refuses unknown keys in layers, surcharges, nails, anchors, geosynthetics, stabilizing piles and the probabilistic variable / correlation specs, naming the item and a did-you-mean (`c` → c_prime or cu, never guessed). All 13 shipped slope parameter sets still pass.
- **GM-48** (downdrag as the owner asked, OWN-01): every method computed on every run and tabulated side by side, each named as its reference names it, with its source (`to_dict()["neutral_plane_comparison"]`, `summary()`, the calc package); one plain sentence gives the spread (`methods_range`); the headline is UFC 3-220-20 §6-7.4's procedure (`headline_method`). The coined wording is gone from results, adapter, calc package and DESIGN, and a test keeps it out. Skin-friction stress defaults to final: GEC-12 §7.3.6.1 gives no rule (its only remark is that stress changes after driving move the neutral plane), so UFC §6-7.4 step 2 governs; both quotes are in DESIGN.md. The geotechnical check below the neutral plane therefore uses the final-stress resistance too. Pile head settlement is now the settlement at the neutral plane plus the elastic compression above it (UFC Fig 6-19); before, it left out the downdrag itself. B-5.5 still matches (47.70 / 50.14 / 52.48 ft against 48 / 50 / 52); V-004 53.61 ft / 490.1 / 289.1 kips with the pile weight in, 54.00 / 486.6 / 285.6 without (published 54 / 486 / 285). Follow-ups GM-49 to GM-51, OWN-58.
- **GM-01** (the ~4 % mismatch between the neutral-plane search and the reported drag force): three integrations of the same friction profile disagreed; now one exact integral of the piecewise-linear profile, and the neutral-plane load equals toe plus positive friction to rounding (pinned by `TestQuadratureConsistency`).
